# Tasks

Every task is self-contained: its **Read** list names what it needs, its **Depends on** line names what must
already exist, and its **Excludes** line names the nearby work that belongs to a different task — so it can be
planned and applied in a session that remembers nothing about the others. These rules apply to every task:

- **Size.** This change is far larger than the ~10-task norm (`AGENTS.md#how-work-is-planned`) because the owner asked
  for it as one change (proposal, assumption 1). Each numbered group is a mergeable unit, and the repository is green
  between groups: the last task of every group ends its **Done when** with `./gradlew build :app:archTest` — every
  module's tests, not only those of the modules the group touched — and any task that changes an `:api` shape, what a
  reader produces, or a bridge between modules runs `./gradlew build` itself rather than its own module's check. Only
  then does a group that depends on it start. Groups marked **∥** in their heading are independent of each other and
  may be worked in any order once their own **Depends on** is met; nothing in two ∥ groups edits the same file.
  Groups 9–16 were re-planned against the code groups 0–8 built, and each of their tasks names the refactoring it
  needs first.
- **Git.** Work on `feature/complete-translation-workflow--<n.m>-<slug>` (for example `--1.1-project-records`), commit
  there, squash-merge one commit into `feature/complete-translation-workflow`, and delete the task branch
  (`AGENTS.md#git`). Never `--no-verify`, never force-push.
- **Tests first.** Write the task's tests, run them and watch them fail, then implement (`AGENTS.md#definition-of-done`).
  Name tests `method_state_expected`, put no `if`, `for` or `while` in a test body (use `@ParameterizedTest`), create
  files in `@TempDir`, and write expected values out instead of recomputing them (`.claude/rules/testing.md`). UI tests
  run on JavaFX 26's built-in headless platform (ADR-0019). "Wired" is asserted behaviourally: drive the state mirror and
  assert the node changed; fire the control and assert a recording fake port was called. No Mockito; WireMock only at
  the HTTP seam, for **both** dialects whenever a provider request changes. A fixture that asserts a segment is
  accepted passes every soft check at full margin under D-3 and the echo floor (below; `design.md` D8, D16): an en→uk
  reply is Cyrillic with a length ratio inside 0.81–1.69 of its source — `Маяк стояв на прибережній скелі.` (ratio 0.97)
  for a 33-character source, never `Маяк стояв на скелі.` (0.61) — while the pseudo model's upper-cased echo of a source
  of 20 or more code points is FLAGGED in every review mode and `Yes, sir.` → `YES, SIR.` is accepted at confidence 0.85.
- **Patterns to copy rather than invent.** TestFX: `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/ShellTestBase.java`,
  `ScreenConformanceTest.java`, `screen/*ScreenTest.java`. Recording fakes: `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/
  Scripted*.java`, `state/RecordingJob.java`, `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/ScriptedChatModel.java`
  and `TranslationJobTestSupport.java`; there is no recording runner — a test that must see the run's control calls uses
  `modules/ui/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/ui/state/RecordingJob.java`'s `calls()`. WireMock: `modules/pipeline/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/WireMockProvider.java`
  (pipeline e2e) and `modules/llm/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/llm/client/ollama/OllamaClientTest.java` /
  `.../openai/OpenAiCompatibleClientTest.java` (one server per dialect). Launch-flag resolution:
  `modules/app/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/app/bootstrap/LoggingLevelResolver.java`.
- **Logging is part of done.** Write what the task's **Log** line lists: INFO for lifecycle, DEBUG for every working
  method's parameters and every branch taken, TRACE for book text, prompts and replies, WARN for degraded, retried or
  refused outcomes, and never a credential (`.claude/rules/logging.md`). Job events carry book text; a log line above
  TRACE never does. No log call inside a layout pass, a cell's `updateItem`, or a listener on a high-frequency value.
- **Code rules.** `.claude/rules/java-coding-style.md`: records for data, `Objects.requireNonNull` at boundaries, a
  `@NullMarked` `package-info.java` with a purpose Javadoc for each new package, constructor injection only with
  `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})` on anything Guice constructs implicitly, enum data in
  constructor fields, no `synchronized`, methods of at most 30 lines and files of at most 400.
  `.claude/rules/error-envelope.md`: every port method returns a `Result` behind a boundary `try/catch`; no new
  `ErrorCode` (ADR-0022, ADR-0039). `.claude/rules/javafx-ui.md` and `.claude/rules/theming-tokens.md`: no scene-graph
  access off the FX thread, no `node.setStyle(...)`, no colour outside a `.root` value block, every visible word from
  the message catalogue in **both** `messages_en` and `messages_uk`.
- **Comments cite behaviour.** A code comment or Javadoc never names a task number — it names the behaviour or the spec
  requirement; a task that touches a file whose comment names a task number rewrites that comment.
- **A new package is not done until JPMS knows about it.** A new `:api`/`:util` package is `exports`-ed; a new package
  holding a class Guice constructs gets `opens <package> to com.google.guice;` — a `:ui` package
  `opens <package> to javafx.fxml, com.google.guice;` — in the task that creates it (`design.md` D1); `:pipeline`'s
  prompt resources need no `opens`, because a module reads its own resources. A missing line passes every classpath
  test and fails only in the packaged image, which is why task 16.2 runs the packaging scripts.
- **Finish** with `./gradlew spotlessApply`, then the task's **Done when** commands.
- **Sources.** Requirements are named in quotes and live in this change's `specs/<capability>/spec.md`, or, for a
  shipped requirement this change leaves as it is, in `openspec/specs/<capability>/spec.md`. Decisions are `design.md`
  D1–D16 and ADR-0034 … ADR-0041. Every number the change fixes — thresholds, margins, caps, temperatures, timeouts —
  is listed once in `design.md` "Tuning constants"; a task that needs one takes it from there. The owner's decisions
  (proposal: What Changes, assumptions 6–8, Non-goals) bind every task:
  - **D-1** — the Providers tab's polish ships: the three test actions with measured badges (tasks 3.4, 15.4).
  - **D-2** — only the text values of Markdown frontmatter become segments: never a key, never `lang` or `language`,
    never a number, a boolean (YAML `yes`/`on` included), a null, a date, a URL, a list or a map; on export an existing
    top-level `lang` value is replaced by the target tag in its own quotes, and a `lang` key is never added.
  - **D-3** — a soft check that fails outright blocks acceptance: it raises a `medium` finding, so the segment is
    repaired and then FLAGGED; confidence decides only close calls.
  - **Echo floor** — a failed echo blocks only when the source's display text has at least 20 code points; below that it
    only lowers confidence (`It was a dark night.` is exactly 20 and blocks).
  - **D-4** — a TXT or Markdown export whose translation the source encoding cannot hold is written whole as UTF-8
    (ADR-0029), never refused; EPUB keeps its numeric character references and FB2 its existing switch.
  - **D-5** — names grow during the run: at each body unit's end a deterministic re-scan adds new unlocked candidates
    with no target, never changing an entry the person kept or bringing back one they removed.
  - **D-6** — kept foreign runs come from metadata only: under the Keep policy an inline element whose own
    `lang`/`xml:lang` differs from the source language is kept verbatim as one protected token.
  - **D-7** — no numeral masking and no automatic model pre-scan (the pre-scan runs only from its button); the reference
    clauses say so (group 0).
  - **D-8** — review stays a panel inside Translating, an approved departure from the mockup.
  - **D-9** — the output cap: a call that states an expected output is capped (Ollama `num_predict`, OpenAI-compatible
    `max_tokens`), `design.md` D6.
  - **D-10** — languages are open: the 34-language catalogue is a convenience list, any language the application can
    name works everywhere (`design.md` D11).
  - **D-11** — one translation per app run: importing while a run can continue asks first (`design.md` D15).
  - **D-12** — a pause or model error inside a segment's repair rounds redoes that segment's rounds from round 1; the
    chunk's judge verdict is kept (`design.md` D4a). Superseded by group 15b, step 1: the rounds already answered are
    kept and the resume continues at the call that failed.

  The visual reference is `docs/specification/mockups/ui-mockup.html`; the crossed-out parts of
  `.temporary_context/screens_of_mockup/Mockup/*.png` (the "Design reference" group and the "Preview state" /
  run-state switcher bars) are never built.

no language is inferred from text; drop the "Lingua" note; cite ADR-0037), FR-IMPORT-06 (the card's rows as task 0.1 lists them, no detected-language row), FR-IMPORT-07 (the cover found by the EPUB 3 cover property, the EPUB 2 cover meta read as an id or an href, the guide's cover page, or the FB2 coverpage; a placeholder otherwise); `#fr-doc` FR-DOC-04 (the protected spans are inline tags, inline code and MathML, locked glossary terms that have a target, URLs and — under the Keep policy — inline runs whose own `lang`/`xml:lang` differs from the source language; no numeral is masked in this build, standalone or in prose — owner decision D-7 — so the "selectively … numerals" clause goes); `#fr-model` FR-MODEL-07 (this build uses one fixed effective context of 8192 tokens, sent to Ollama as `num_ctx`; OpenAI-compatible servers receive no context field; resolving a per-provider value from `/api/show`, discovery or a provider field arrives with the Generation settings); `#fr-brief` FR-BRIEF-01 (source and target both chosen from one searchable list of 34 languages; the source preselected from the normalized declaration or the content majority; the same language on both sides is refused); `#fr-algo` FR-ALGO-02 (`effectiveBudget = min(8192 − reserved headroom, 1200)`; a chunk is consecutive segments of one unit, capped by the dial at 8 / 4 / 2 segments and at 1 under Manual review; cite ADR-0038) and FR-ALGO-09 (each draft is one model call for one segment answering exactly `{"target":"…"}`; there is no JSON array keyed by segment id and no id-mismatch fallback; cite ADR-0038); `#fr-export` FR-EXPORT-04 (the save path, Browse and overwrite are chosen on the Export screen, export runs at any time except while translating, a finished export is confirmed in a dialog; cite ADR-0035); `#fr-review` FR-REVIEW-01 (Unattended never pauses for review, Assisted pauses on each flagged segment and on errors, Manual pauses after every segment; τ 0.60 / 0.75 / 0.85; the mode comes from a launch flag until Settings persist; cite ADR-0036), FR-REVIEW-02 (τ comes from the review mode alone; the Settings override is not in this build), FR-REVIEW-05 (a retry replays the context the segment's first draft saw, runs no repair rounds, and is allowed whenever no run of the project is running — refused as busy, with no model call, while one is), FR-REVIEW-06 (Accept moves FLAGGED to ACCEPTED and confirms an ACCEPTED segment; on a PENDING or REVISED segment it is refused and changes nothing) and FR-REVIEW-09 (editing the target disables Accept until Save edit or Revert; Save edit records REVISED and keeps the machine target apart); `#fr-gloss` FR-GLOSS-01 (glossary proposals come from a deterministic frequency and capitalisation scan — when a run prepares on an empty glossary, when Names & style opens on an empty glossary, and at each body unit's end — unlocked, with no target and gender unknown; the LLM pre-scan runs only when the person presses its button, never at a run's start; owner decision D-7, cite DD-46); `#fr-resume` FR-RESUME-04 (a source file changed since the book was opened is detected by its hash when the book is exported, which is refused with `validation` and writes nothing; resuming within the session does not re-read the source) and FR-RESUME-05 (Stop aborts the request in flight and ends the run `stopped`, never as an error; progress is kept for the session and a new run for the same book starts at its first PENDING segment; nothing survives closing the application); `#fr-qa` FR-QA-03 (the target-language check is a script check — the share of letters in the target language's script, run only for sources of at least 20 characters between languages of different scripts — and under the Keep policy a segment whose declared block language differs from the run's source language, or whose dominant script differs from the source language's, has the echo and script checks skipped) and FR-QA-07 (a **segment**, not a chunk, is accepted when its hard gates pass, no soft check failed outright — a failed echo on a source under 20 code points only lowers confidence — confidence ≥ τ, and the judge is off or scored the chunk at τ_judge or above with no medium or high finding against that segment; a context-matched memory reuse is accepted without the judge; cite ADR-0038). In `docs/specification/01_Product/05_TRANSLATION_ALGORITHM.md`: `#phase-b-prep` FR-ALGO-B1 (preparation runs the deterministic scan when the glossary is empty; the LLM pre-scan is the person's button only, in batches of 40 candidates, merged only when every batch succeeded), `#token-budget` FR-ALGO-C1 (`chunkBudget = min(8192 − reservedHeadroom, 1200)`), `#phase-d-backward-revision` FR-ALGO-D2 (the deterministic sweep replaces a rendering only where the glossary held a previous target for that term, recorded when the person changed it; a rendering the model chose for a term that had no target is not known to the sweep). In `docs/specification/01_Product/03_DOCUMENT_FORMATS.md#drm-and-language-detection`, EC-LANG-1 and EC-LANG-2 say the same as FR-IMPORT-03 (metadata evidence, no text detection), EC-FOREIGN-1 says a block is foreign when it declares, on itself or an ancestor below the document root, a language other than the run's source language, or its dominant script differs from the source language's — nothing is detected from text — and the foreign-passage policy applies (keep by default), and EC-FOREIGN-3 says that under the Keep policy an inline element whose own `lang`/`xml:lang` differs from the source language is kept verbatim as one protected token while the prose around it is translated, and that an unmarked foreign run is not detected (owner decision D-6; cite ADR-0037). In `docs/specification/00_Foundation/04_DESIGN_DECISIONS.md`: `#dd-40-prompt-evals` (a note that `complete-translation-workflow` ships no `promptEval` harness — its prompts are pinned by a prompt-golden test and `liveLocal` cases — and the evals stay planned), `#dd-44-token-budget-heuristic` (the effective context is the fixed 8192 in this build; the per-provider resolution is not built), `#dd-45-acceptance-model` (the judge scores a chunk once and a segment is accepted only when its hard gates pass, no soft check failed outright — a failed check raises a medium finding and sends the segment to repair, the echo floor of 20 code points excepted — confidence reaches τ, and the chunk score reaches τ_judge with no medium or high finding against that segment; τ lives on the review mode; the manual Settings override is not in this build; cite ADR-0038, ADR-0036), `#dd-46-glossary-llm-pre-scan` (the deterministic scan seeds the glossary and grows it at each body unit's end; the LLM pre-scan runs only from its button and never at a run's start — owner decision D-7), `#dd-47-metadata-nav-alt-translation` (the auxiliary unit is always produced whatever the switches; descriptions — the EPUB `dc:description` and the FB2 `annotation` — are a `METADATA_DESCRIPTION` kind under the metadata switch; each XHTML page's `<head><title>` is a `TITLE` segment under the navigation switch; a switched-off kind is kept as source by choice; cite ADR-0041), `#dd-49-code-and-technical-content-preservation` (drop "selective numerals" from the masked list) and `#dd-30-export-original-format` (export is its own action, runnable at any time including on a partial book, still same-format only; cite ADR-0035). In `docs/specification/01_Product/02_TRANSLATION_WORKFLOW.md`: `#end-to-end-workflow` (the Names & style row: proposals come from the deterministic scan and grow during the run; the LLM pre-scan is a button, never automatic), `#review-modes` (the table's behaviour column says what each mode pauses on and its τ; Manual packs one segment per chunk) and `#workflow-states-and-recovery` (a provider error auto-pauses with Retry now; a user Stop keeps progress for the session; crash or quit loses the session until persistence exists; a changed source is detected at export, not before a resume). In `docs/specification/01_Product/07_SETTINGS.md`: `#generation-tab` — the `num_ctx` row and the chunk-budget paragraph say this build sends a fixed 8192 and uses no per-provider effective context, the 32768 default applying once the Generation tab is built; `#automation-tab` — the "Default review mode" row says the value comes from `BOOKLOOM_REVIEW_MODE` or `bookloom.review.mode` until Settings are persisted. In `docs/specification/01_Product/06_REVIEW_AND_EDITING.md#segment-actions`, add the Revert row, say that Accept acts on FLAGGED and ACCEPTED only and that Retry is allowed whenever no run of the project is running, and scope FR-REVIEW-A3 ("persists atomically") to the session.

- [x] 0.4 Restate the invariant statements this change widens or corrects — "only text nodes change" becomes "only text nodes and the attribute values DD-47 lists change" (image alt text, ADR-0041); the placeholder rule gains "pairs keep their order and nesting, a pair that held text still holds text, a line break stays inside its pair, while atomic tokens may move" (ADR-0040 as amended) and loses selective numerals (owner decision D-7); and the single-flight rule says the gate **blocks** — no call is refused by it, and a review retry asked while the project's run is RUNNING is refused as busy from the run record, never queued behind it — instead of a `tryAcquire`/`tryRun` that does not exist; the provider rule sends one fixed context of 8192 and reads no `/api/show`. the "Single-flight inference" invariant → one `InferenceGate` (`Semaphore(1)`) that blocks and guards all inference, runs and diagnostics alike — no call is refused by it; the one `busy` is a review retry asked while the project's run is RUNNING, refused from the run record and never queued behind the run (`design.md` D9) — keeping its backoff, retry and timeout sentences. `.claude/rules/document-roundtrip.md`: the first MUST names the alt exception and ADR-0041; the masking MUST masks inline tags, locked terms and URLs (no numerals, owner decision D-7) and says the gate checks the multiset **and** that paired tokens stay opening-before-closing and properly nested, that a pair whose source held text still holds text, and that a line-break token keeps its innermost enclosing pair, while an atomic token (image, code span, locked term, kept foreign run) may move (ADR-0040 as amended), with its rationale reworded to match; the Reject-if line "Placeholders are dropped, added, reordered" becomes "dropped, added, duplicated, a pair reversed or overlapped, a pair emptied of its text, or a line break moved across a pair boundary, instead of failing the hard gate". `.claude/rules/threading-concurrency.md`: the gate MUST says the semaphore blocks, a failed acquire does not exist, and a review retry is refused with `ErrorCode.busy` while the project's run is RUNNING and is never queued behind it. `.claude/rules/llm-provider-integration.md`: the gate MUST says `run` blocks and there is no `tryRun`, with the review retry's `busy` coming from the run record; the credential pre-check MUST says diagnostics go through the same blocking gate and wait for a call in flight; the Reject-if line becomes "Inference runs without acquiring the `InferenceGate`, or a review retry is queued behind a RUNNING run instead of being refused with `busy`"; the reasoning MUST loses "informed by `/api/show`" (the Ollama request carries `think:false` when reasoning is off and never reads `/api/show`); the effective-context SHOULD says this build sends one fixed context of 8192 as Ollama `num_ctx`, sends none to an OpenAI-compatible server, and resolves no per-provider value until the Generation settings exist. `.agents/skills/llm-provider-integration/SKILL.md`: step 8, the checklist line and the Gotchas line say the same (`run` only; `busy` from the run record for a review retry). `docs/specification/02_Architecture/08_THREADING_CONCURRENCY.md#gate-interplay`: the "Interactive 'retry now' uses `tryRun`" bullet says the same. `docs/specification/02_Architecture/04_LLM_INTEGRATION.md#inference-gate`: the sentence deferring "Interactive `tryRun` and the `ErrorCode.busy` UI behavior" says there is no `tryRun` and names the review retry's `busy`. `docs/specification/01_Product/04_LLM_PROVIDERS_AND_MODELS.md`: `#three-stage-verification`'s bounded-wait `tryRun` sentence and EC-CONCUR-2 in `#provider-edge-cases` say verification goes through the same blocking gate, so a test during a run waits for the call in flight.

Every storage port, the review desk, export and the reworked job read and write these shapes; the brief's defaults are what a newly opened book starts with, so getting one wrong silently changes every translation; and a record that kept only the plain user edit, or only whether a run had ended, would refuse a second edit of a formatted segment and a retry during a pause. → `:api`

`reviewed` is true once a person accepted, saved, reverted, applied a proposal or retried successfully — with `effectiveTarget() → Optional<String>` (the user's edit when present, else the machine target), `isKeptAsSource(Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → boolean` (true exactly for a record of the auxiliary unit whose kind is in the set, whatever its status — the one rule every repository read and count applies), and `with…` copy methods for status, machine targets (plain and masked together), user targets (plain and masked together), findings, path and reviewed; `SegmentLocator(String text)` (a record, so `:api` stays free of Lombok and every framework) with the factory `static SegmentLocator of(SegmentKind kind, int unitOrdinal, int segmentOrdinal)` — `unitOrdinal` 0 is the auxiliary unit and body units count from 1; a body segment reads `ch<n> · p<mm>` with `n` its unit's position among body units and `mm` its 1-based position in its unit, zero-padded to two digits (`ch5 · p12`, `ch1 · p03`); an auxiliary segment reads by its kind — `METADATA_TITLE` → `title`, `METADATA_AUTHOR` → `author · <kk>`, `METADATA_DESCRIPTION` → `description · <kk>`, `NAV_LABEL` → `nav · <kk>` (NCX labels included), `TITLE` → `page title · <kk>`, `ALT` → `alt · <kk>`, `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` → `frontmatter · <kk>`, with `kk` = `segmentOrdinal`, its 1-based position among the auxiliary segments of that kind, two digits — the one rule the review panel, toasts, the log and the export report use (`design.md` D2); `GlossaryEntry(String id, String projectId, String term, @Nullable String target, TermType type, Gender gender, boolean locked)`; `TmEntry(String id, String projectId, String sourceHash, String contextKey, String sourceInner, String targetInner)`; - [x] 1.3 Add the storage ports to `:api` in a new exported package `ua.bookloom.api.persistence` — project, segment, glossary, translation-memory, summary, deferral and run repositories and one checkpoint port that commits a chunk's decided segments — every method returning `Result`, with the segment reads taking the auxiliary kinds the brief keeps as source, the glossary remembering a term the person removed, runs saved by upsert, and a commit that skips a glossary addition instead of failing. Review, export, retry and resume must read one source of truth that SQLite can later replace without touching a caller (ADR-0034); a port that throws or returns a bare value would make the in-memory adapter the only possible implementation, and a commit that failed on a duplicate name would throw away a chunk's decisions because the person added that name during a pause. → `:api`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.2 (the records these ports carry, `SegmentCounts` and `SegmentRecord.isKeptAsSource` included).

`countsByStatus(String projectId, Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → Result<SegmentCounts>` — every record counted once, a record kept as source only as `sourceKept` whatever its status; `flagged(String projectId, Set<SegmentKind> keptAuxiliaryKinds) → Result<List<SegmentRecord>>` in document order, kept records left out; ## 2. ∥ In-memory storage — after 1.2 and 1.3

- [x] 2.1 Implement the project and segment repositories in memory in a new package `ua.bookloom.persistence.memory`, over one shared store, and write the abstract `RepositoryContractTest` against the ports so the SQLite change reuses it unchanged. Every screen and the job read the segment repository — first pending, counts by status, the flagged list, one unit's segments — and each read must leave out, or count apart, the auxiliary segments the brief keeps as source by choice; these reads must be correct and atomic before anything is built on them. → `:persistence`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 1.2 and 1.3.
  - **Excludes:** the glossary, TM, summary, deferral and run repositories (task 2.2), the chunk checkpoint and its locking (task 2.3), Guice bindings and `module-info` (task 2.4); deciding which kinds are kept (the brief's `AlsoTranslate.keptKinds()`, read by the job and the screens).
  - **Read:** ADR-0034; `design.md` D2 (concurrent maps, atomic per-key updates, kept as source by choice, the shared contract test); `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "Move segments only through the segment status machine" ("A flagged segment is not picked up again by the run"); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Skip the auxiliary text the brief switches off" ("A switch turned off after a run keeps its segments as source"); `specs/resume/spec.md` "Keep a run's progress only until the application closes"; the two port files `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/persistence/{ProjectRepository,SegmentRepository}.java` (task 1.3); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/project/SegmentRecord.java` (task 1.2; `isKeptAsSource`); `modules/persistence/build.gradle.kts` (the test conventions are applied; the module has no `src/test` yet); `.claude/rules/error-envelope.md` (boundary `try/catch` on every port method); `.claude/rules/testing.md`.
  - **Change:** `InMemoryStore` (package-private state holder, one per injector): per-project maps of projects and segment records, each segment remembering its insertion index as its document order, and one `ReentrantReadWriteLock` that multi-record reads take for reading and task 2.3's checkpoint takes for writing. `InMemoryProjectRepository` and `InMemorySegmentRepository` implement the ports over it; `firstPending`, `countsByStatus` and `flagged` apply `SegmentRecord.isKeptAsSource` with the set the caller passes, never a set of their own; `update` uses the map's `compute`; unknown project or segment → `Result.err(validation)`; any escaping `Throwable` → `Result.err(internal)`; constructors `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})`. `package-info.java` with `@NullMarked` and a purpose Javadoc. The contract test lives in `modules/persistence/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/persistence/contract/RepositoryContractTest.java` (abstract, with factory methods for each port; created by this task) and `modules/persistence/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/persistence/memory/InMemoryRepositoryContractTest.java` (created by this task) extends it — the SQLite adapter will live in the same module, so the test source set is shared without a test-fixtures plugin, which no module in this build uses.
  - **Test first:** in the contract test (repository contract tier), with the records `ch1:0`, `ch1:1` (unit `ch1`), `ch2:0` (unit `ch2`), `aux:title` (unit `aux`, `METADATA_TITLE`) and `aux:fm:title` (unit `aux`, `FRONTMATTER_VALUE`) saved PENDING in that order and the kept set `{FRONTMATTER_VALUE}`: a saved project is found by id and an unknown id answers ok-and-empty; `firstPending` = `ch1:0`; after `ch1:0` is updated to ACCEPTED, `firstPending` = `ch1:1` and `countsByStatus` = `SegmentCounts(pending=3, accepted=1, revised=0, flagged=0, sourceKept=1)`; after `ch1:1` becomes FLAGGED, `flagged` returns exactly `[ch1:1]` and `firstPending` = `ch2:0` (a flagged segment is never picked up again); after `ch2:0` is ACCEPTED, `firstPending` = `aux:title` and never `aux:fm:title`; read with the empty kept set, `firstPending` after `aux:title` is ACCEPTED is `aux:fm:title`; `aux:fm:title` made FLAGGED and read again with `{FRONTMATTER_VALUE}` is absent from `flagged` and counted only in `sourceKept`; `byUnit("ch1")` returns `ch1:0`, `ch1:1` in order; updating unknown `ch9:0` answers `ErrorCode.validation`; records of project `p1` are invisible to project `p2`. Concurrency: two threads each applying 1,000 updates that increment `repairRounds` on `ch1:0` end at `2000`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG on each write (project id, segment id or count, the status moved from and to) and on each branch that answers an error (the id and the code); no DEBUG in the pure reads (`find`, `countsByStatus`, `flagged`, `firstPending`), which run per segment (`docs/next_features.md` §12); never a target text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :persistence:check` is green with branch coverage at or above the core floor.

- [x] 2.2 Implement the glossary, translation-memory, summary, deferral and run repositories in memory over the same store, with case-insensitive glossary terms and a memory of the terms the person removed, one TM entry per source hash and context key, exact, context and fuzzy-candidate TM lookups, and runs saved by upsert so each run's current state is one record. The glossary's duplicate rule is what the Add term dialog relies on and its removal memory is what stops the chapter-end scan bringing a deleted name back; the TM lookups decide whether a segment is reused without a model call — a wrong key here silently reuses a translation in the wrong context; and a review retry is allowed or refused from the latest run's state. → `:persistence`
  - **Depends on:** task 2.1 (the store and the contract test).
  - **Excludes:** the checkpoint (task 2.3); fuzzy similarity scoring and context-key hashing (task 9.7 in `:pipeline`) — the repository only filters candidates by source length; writing the run states during a run (tasks 10.3, 10.4).
  - **Read:** `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Add a term through a dialog that refuses duplicates", "Import and export the glossary as CSV", "Keep the person's entries and removals when names are proposed"; `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Reuse the translation memory only where its context matches", "Keep a rolling summary of the book so far", "Record deferrals and revise backwards on Max"; `specs/resume/spec.md` "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended"; `design.md` D2 (the removal memory, the run upsert), D10; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/06_DATA_MODEL_SQLITE.md#ddl-normative` (the `UNIQUE` constraints of `glossary`, `tm`, `deferrals`); the five port files in `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/persistence/` (task 1.3).
  - **Change:** `InMemoryGlossaryRepository` (term uniqueness by `toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)` per project, insertion order kept; `remove` records the lower-cased term in a per-project removed set, `wasRemoved` reads it, `add` clears it for that term), `InMemoryTmRepository` (key: project, source hash, context key; `put` replaces), `InMemorySummaryRepository` (latest by highest version), `InMemoryDeferralRepository` (idempotent on project, segment, reason), `InMemoryRunRepository` (upsert by run id; `latest` = the record with the latest `startedAt`); each with the boundary `try/catch` and `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})`. The contract test gains the matching abstract factory methods.
  - **Test first:** contract-test cases: adding `Hale` then `hale` answers `ErrorCode.validation` and leaves one entry; `findByTerm("HALE")` finds `Hale`; updating its target to `Гейл` is kept; removing it empties the list and `wasRemoved(p1, "HALE")` is true; adding `hale` again clears the memory (`wasRemoved` false) and holds one entry; a removal in `p1` is not remembered for `p2`. TM: `put(h1, c1, "Hello", "Привіт")` and `put(h1, c2, "Hello", "Вітаю")` give `exact(h1)` of size 2 and `context(h1, c2)` = `Вітаю`; a second `put(h1, c1, …, "Здоров")` replaces the first (still 2 entries); `candidates(p1, 3, 6)` returns the entries whose source is 3–6 characters and excludes a 20-character one. Summary: versions 1 then 2 saved → `latest` is version 2. Deferral: adding `(ch1:4, GENDER_UNKNOWN)` twice keeps one; `resolve` removes it from `open`. Run (resume "Record whether a run is running, paused or ended"): saving run `r1` RUNNING, then `r1` PAUSED, `r1` RUNNING and `r1` COMPLETED with an end time leaves one record whose state `latest(p1)` reports after each save as RUNNING, PAUSED, RUNNING, COMPLETED ("The state follows pauses and resumes"); a run saved CANCELLED reads as stopped ("A stopped run is recorded as stopped") and one saved FAILED as failed ("A failed run is recorded as failed"); a run `r2` started after `r1` is `latest`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG on each write (project id, entry id, term length or key hashes, the decision taken — added, replaced, refused as duplicate, removal remembered or cleared; run id and state); no DEBUG in pure reads; never a term, source or target text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :persistence:check` is green.

- [x] 2.3 Implement `InMemoryCheckpoint.commit(ChunkCommit)` so a chunk's segment records, TM entries, deferrals and new glossary names become visible all together or not at all, skipping a glossary addition whose term already exists or was removed by the person, refusing only a segment the project does not hold, and prove it with a two-thread visibility test. A reader such as the review panel or a progress count that saw half a chunk would show a segment ACCEPTED whose TM entry does not yet exist, a failure in the middle would leave the book in a state no resume can explain (ADR-0034, DD-20), and a commit that failed because the person added a name during a pause would throw away the chunk's decisions. → `:persistence`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 2.1 and 2.2.
  - **Excludes:** bindings (task 2.4); deciding what goes into a commit (tasks 10.3–10.11).
  - **Read:** ADR-0034 (as amended: commits hold decided segments); `design.md` D2 (one lock so a commit is never half-visible; the commit skips an existing or removed term); `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Keep the person's entries and removals when names are proposed" ("A chapter-end scan leaves the person's entry alone", "A removed term is not proposed again"); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Propose new names at the end of each body unit"; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/08_THREADING_CONCURRENCY.md` (no `synchronized`); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/persistence/CheckpointPort.java` (task 1.3); `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/project/ChunkCommit.java` (task 1.2).
  - **Change:** `InMemoryCheckpoint` takes the store's write lock, validates first that every segment record exists and belongs to the commit's project, then applies the segment records, TM entries and deferrals, and adds each glossary addition whose term the project neither holds (ignoring case) nor has in its removed set — skipping the others with the existing entry untouched — releasing in `finally`; an unknown segment applies nothing and answers `ErrorCode.validation`; the answer is the number of records applied (segments, TM entries, deferrals and glossary entries added); the multi-record reads of task 2.1 already take the read lock.
  - **Test first:** repository contract tier: a commit of two segment updates, one TM entry and one deferral answers `4` and all four are then readable; a commit whose second segment id is unknown answers `validation` and leaves the first segment PENDING and no TM entry stored; a commit adding the glossary term `Hale` when the person's `hale` → `Гейл` (character, male, locked) exists applies its segments and TM entry, keeps that entry exactly as it was, adds no second entry and answers the count without the skipped addition; a commit adding `Chapter` after the person removed `Chapter` in this session applies its segments and adds no `Chapter`; a commit adding the new term `Milton` adds it unlocked with no target. Concurrency: a writer thread commits 500 chunks, each moving two segments PENDING → ACCEPTED together, while a reader thread calls `countsByStatus` 5,000 times — every observed accepted count is even (no half-applied chunk is ever seen). Written against the port in `RepositoryContractTest`.
  - **Log:** DEBUG per commit (project id, counts of each list, how many glossary additions were skipped as existing or removed, the outcome); DEBUG on a refused commit naming the first offending id and the code; the skipped terms at TRACE only; INFO nowhere (a commit is per chunk, not a lifecycle event).
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :persistence:check` is green and `grep -rn "synchronized" modules/persistence/src/main` prints nothing.

## 3. ∥ Provider clients — after 1.5

- [x] 3.1 Make the Ollama-native client read token usage from `prompt_eval_count`, `eval_count` and `eval_duration`, and send the request's context size as `options.num_ctx`, leaving the `options` object out only when neither a temperature nor a context size is given, and prove that a request `GatedChatModel` sends again without a rejected control or format still posts that context size. Ollama's default context silently truncates a long prompt (proposal assumption 4), a downgraded retry that lost `num_ctx` would bring that truncation back, and without usage figures the Translating screen can only estimate its speed. → `:llm`
  - **Depends on:** task 1.5 (`TokenUsage`, `ChatResponse.usage`, `ChatRequest.contextWindow`, the copy methods `GatedChatModel` now uses).

  - **Change:** `OllamaChatRequest.Options` gains `@JsonProperty("num_ctx") @Nullable Integer numCtx`; the compact constructor drops `options` only when both fields are null. `OllamaChatResponse` gains `prompt_eval_count`, `eval_count` (`@Nullable Integer`) and `eval_duration` (`@Nullable Long`, nanoseconds). `OllamaClient` copies `contextWindow` into `numCtx` and builds `TokenUsage` from the three fields when at least one is present (`Duration.ofNanos`), else no usage.

- [x] 3.3 Replace the fixed per-request timeout of a chat call with one scaled to the request's expected output in both clients — `max(configured, min(600 s, expected output tokens × 0.5 s))`, the configured request timeout (180 s by default, or the command line's `--timeout`) always the floor — keeping the configured timeout for a request that states no expected output. A fixed three-minute wait can be too short for a page from a slow model, while cutting a short call below the configured timeout would time out the first call after Ollama reloads a model for a larger context, or a prompt read on a CPU, and pause the run for nothing (`docs/next_features.md` §15). → `:llm`

  - **Change:** `ua.bookloom.llm.http.RequestTimeouts` (static utility) with `forChat(ProviderConfig config, @Nullable Integer expectedOutputTokens) → ProviderConfig`: when an expectation is present, `config.withRequestTimeout(max(config.requestTimeout(), min(600 s, expected × 500 ms)))`, else `config` unchanged; named constants for 500 ms per token and the 600 s ceiling. Both clients post chat requests with the returned config, so the existing timeout mapping names the effective timeout in `details`. No lower clamp exists: the configured timeout is the floor.
  - **Test first:** `RequestTimeoutsTest` as a `@CsvSource` of configured seconds, expected tokens → effective seconds: `180,40`→`180`, `180,400`→`200`, `180,1200`→`600`, `180,2000`→`600`, `30,40`→`30`, `30,400`→`200`, `900,2000`→`900`; a null expectation with `180` configured returns `180`. WireMock delay tests, **both dialects** (`/api/chat` and `/v1/chat/completions`), with a configured request timeout of 1 s and a stub delayed 1.5 s: a request expecting 400 tokens gets its reply (the scaled 200 s replaced the configured 1 s); a request expecting 1 token keeps the 1 s floor and fails with `ErrorCode.timeout` whose details contain `timeoutMs=1000`; a request stating no expectation fails the same way. In `GatedChatModelTest` (the dialect `chat_structuredOutputRejected_retriesOnceWithoutResponseFormat` uses), a request expecting 400 tokens whose format is rejected at once and whose retry is answered after 1.5 s, with a configured timeout of 1 s, gets its reply — the downgraded request kept its expected output.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the expected output tokens, the configured and the effective timeout, and which branch (scaled above the floor, floor kept, or no expectation) decided it.

The tab shows "reachable · 41 ms", "3 models", "inference · 1.2 s" (owner decision D-1); without measurement those badges have nothing to say, and without the shorter policies "Test connection" would have to call a model it may not have. → `:api`, `:llm`

- [x] 4.2 Implement `BookInspector.inspect(Path)` as the one public façade `inspect.BookInspectorService` over four per-format inspections, answering readable, DRM-protected with the encryption scheme named from `META-INF/encryption.xml` where it can be identified — or `ZIP encryption` for a zipped FB2 whose entry is encrypted — or unsupported with the file type detected from the extension and the leading bytes, together with the format, its version and the language evidence, and record in the EPUB parser the manifest ids and properties, the package version, the cover meta, the guide and the spine's `toc` that inspection needs — without changing what `DocumentPort.open` returns for any file. The import screen must show "Adobe ADEPT — Import blocked" and "PDF — not supported" as distinct states, and both used to arrive as the same validation error told apart only by message text (ADR-0039); the command line's exit codes must not move; and the parser classes inspection needs are package-private, so the inspection of each format has to live inside that format's package. → `:document`

  - **Change:** `inspect.FormatInspection` (interface: `inspect(Path) → BookInspection` for a file of its format, and the profile parts tasks 4.3–4.5 add) implemented by `epub.EpubInspection`, `fb2.Fb2Inspection`, `md.MarkdownInspection` and `txt.TxtInspection` — each public, `@RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})`, inside its non-exported format package, so `OpfParser`, `ParsedEpub`, `ParsedFb2`, `Frontmatter` and the rest stay package-private — and `inspect.BookInspectorService` (`implements BookInspector`) choosing the inspection by format. `FormatResolver` moves to `ua.bookloom.document.model` as a public class with a public `resolve`, which `DocumentService` keeps calling (that package is not exported, so nothing outside `:document` sees it), and gains a non-throwing `probe(Path) → Optional<BookFormat>` the façade uses. The façade: a supported format → its inspection; otherwise `UNSUPPORTED` with `PDF` (bytes begin `%PDF-`), `DOCX` (a ZIP holding `[Content_Types].xml`), `MOBI` (`BOOKMOBI` at byte 60) or `Unknown`. `DrmAdjudicator` gains a non-throwing query answering whether content (not font-obfuscated fonts) is encrypted and which scheme: `Adobe ADEPT` when an `EncryptedData`'s `KeyInfo` is in the `http://ns.adobe.com/adept` namespace or `META-INF/rights.xml` is in it, `Readium LCP` when `META-INF/license.lcpl` exists, `Apple FairPlay` when `META-INF/sinf.xml` exists, otherwise none; `adjudicate` is rebuilt on it and still throws for `open`. `EpubInspection`: supported and not encrypted → `READABLE` with `EPUB ` + the package `version` (e.g. `EPUB 2.0`); encrypted → `DRM_PROTECTED` with the scheme or null; a container that cannot be read (a truncated ZIP) → `READABLE` with no version, so `open` supplies the reason. `Fb2Inspection`: `FB2`; a `.fb2.zip` whose entry carries the zip encryption flag (`model.ZipEncryption`) → `DRM_PROTECTED` with the scheme `ZIP encryption`. `MarkdownInspection` → `Markdown`, `TxtInspection` → `TXT`. Each fills its `LanguageEvidence` through `LanguageEvidenceReader`. Parser additions in `epub`: `ManifestItem(String id, String href, String mediaType, Set<String> properties)`; `ParsedOpf` records the package `version`, the `<meta name="cover">` content, the guide references (type and href) and the spine's `toc` attribute — read by tasks 4.3 and 4.4. Boundary `try/catch` → `internal`. `DocumentService.open` is not changed.

  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:check` is green and `./gradlew :app:test --tests '*TranslateCommand*'` passes unchanged.

Golden: `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` stays green (canonical-equal apart from the language metadata), and a new `GoldenComparisonMetaTest` case proves a changed `class` attribute on `<html>` is still reported.

  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and, closing the group, `./gradlew build :app:archTest` are green.

## 5. Write fidelity and splitting — after group 4 (the same reader and writer files)

- [x] 5.1 Write the EPUB package document with line-feed line ends only, and with an XML declaration only when the source package document had one, keeping the encoding it named. The OPF gained a declaration it never had and carriage returns it never had, and the golden comparison could not see it because it re-serializes both sides the same way (`docs/next_features.md` §2) — a file that changes where nothing was translated is what "canonical-equal" is meant to rule out. → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 4.6 (the last group-4 edit of `EpubWriter`).
  - **Excludes:** the XHTML content documents (tasks 5.2, 5.3); the navigation document and the NCX, which task 6.3 re-serializes under this same rule.
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Keep the package document's line ends and declaration"; `docs/next_features.md` §2; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/EpubWriter.java` (`serializeOpf`: `new XMLOutputter(Format.getRawFormat())`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{OpfParser,ParsedOpf}.java`; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/model/Jdom2TreeNode.java` (the LF separator pinned there); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/EpubCanonicalAssert.java` (why the comparator cannot see it).
  - **Change:** `OpfParser` records in `ParsedOpf` whether the source bytes began with an XML declaration and the encoding it named; `serializeOpf` uses a raw format with `LineSeparator.UNIX`, omits the declaration when the source had none, and otherwise writes it with the recorded encoding (and encodes the bytes in it). The rule is written as one helper the NCX serialization of task 6.3 calls too.
  - **Test first:** `EpubWriterTest` asserting the written `OEBPS/content.opf` bytes directly (the comparator cannot): a source package with no declaration and only LF, written with zero edits, has no `<?xml` prefix and no `0x0D` byte; a source beginning `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>` begins with a declaration naming `UTF-8`. The per-format golden stays green.
  - **Log:** DEBUG whether the source had a declaration, its encoding, and the choice made when writing.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [x] 5.2 Keep a line feed, carriage return or tab inside an XHTML attribute value as a character reference when writing, through a serialization post-pass, and make the canonical comparator normalize attribute values the way an XML reader does, so the golden test can see this class of change. An XML reader turns a raw line feed in an attribute into a space, so writing `&#10;` back as a raw line feed silently changes the value — one surveyed book loses a line break in a bookmark label (`docs/next_features.md` §3) — and jsoup cannot keep the reference through its tree, so the only place to put it back is the serialized output. → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.1.
  - **Excludes:** translating attribute values (group 6).
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Keep line-feed references in attribute values"; `design.md` D14 §3 (the post-pass); `docs/next_features.md` §3 (the `data-pdf-bookmark` evidence); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/epub/{XhtmlParser,EpubWriter,PreformattedLineFeedRestorer}.java` (jsoup with `syntax(xml)`); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/{EpubCanonicalAssert,GoldenComparisonMetaTest}.java`.
  - **Change:** content-document serialization, on the serialization copy only (never the registry-held tree): replace each `\n`, `\r` and `\t` inside an attribute value with the private-use sentinels U+E00A, U+E00D and U+E009, serialize, then replace each sentinel in the output string with `&#10;`, `&#13;` and `&#9;`; a document whose source already contains one of those sentinel code points anywhere skips the post-pass, with one DEBUG line, and is written as jsoup writes it today. `EpubCanonicalAssert` reads both sides with XML attribute-value normalization, so a raw line feed and a reference no longer compare equal.
  - **Test first:** `EpubWriterTest`: an element `data-pdf-bookmark="Communication: &#10;So Many Choices"` written with zero edits carries a line-feed reference in the output bytes, and re-reading the output with an XML parser yields a value containing `\n`, not a space; a document that already contains U+E00A in its text skips the post-pass and writes that attribute's line feed raw. `GoldenComparisonMetaTest`: an output that writes that attribute with a raw line feed is reported as a difference (red before the comparator change, the proof the golden now sees it). The golden suite stays green.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the count of attribute values escaped per document, and a skipped post-pass with its reason (no values; attribute text is book text and goes to TRACE only).
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [x] 5.3 Keep an XHTML content document's prolog — its XML declaration and DOCTYPE — verbatim and write it ahead of the serialized document, instead of letting jsoup turn the declaration into a `<!--?xml … ?-->` comment and normalize the DOCTYPE's quotes and line breaks. For a UTF-8 chapter the comment is harmless; for a chapter in any other encoding the reader no longer learns the encoding and decodes it wrongly (`docs/next_features.md` §4). → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.2.
  - **Excludes:** transcoding (an EPUB never needs it — jsoup writes numeric character references, ADR-0029 as amended; TXT and Markdown are task 5.5); the navigation document, which task 6.3 writes under this same rule.

  - **Change:** at parse time record, per spine document, the source prolog bytes (everything before the root element's start tag); at write time emit that prolog followed by the serialized root element, with jsoup's own declaration and DOCTYPE suppressed.

The golden suite (including `pg2760`-style fixtures in `EpubFixtures`) stays green.
  - **Log:** DEBUG per document whether a prolog was recorded and its length in bytes.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

The encoding rules already say a mark is recorded and re-emitted exactly as found; plain text does that and FB2 dropped it, and some readers rely on the mark to choose the encoding (`docs/next_features.md` §5). → `:document`

- [x] 5.5 Write a TXT or Markdown export whose translation the encoding resolved at import cannot hold as UTF-8 — the whole document, with a byte-order mark exactly when the source had one and UTF-8 recorded as the document's charset — instead of refusing it (TXT) or writing `?` (Markdown). The refusal threw away a finished translation over a code-page detail the person cannot change — a `windows-1252` book translated into Ukrainian has almost no representable character — and the `?` destroyed the text silently; switching the whole file keeps every character, as FB2 always has (owner decision D-4, ADR-0029 as amended, `docs/next_features.md` §6 and §10). → `:document`
  - **Depends on:** task 5.4.
  - **Excludes:** EPUB, which writes a numeric character reference and never switches (task 5.6 pins it); FB2, whose switch is unchanged; the zero-edit goldens, which stay as they are.
  - **Read:** `specs/document-round-trip/spec.md` "Write a TXT or Markdown export as UTF-8 when its encoding cannot hold the translation" (every scenario), "Reassemble plain text by splicing target spans into the original bytes", "Reassemble Markdown by splicing translated spans into the original bytes", "Pass a golden round-trip test for TXT", and the removed "Refuse a plain-text export the source encoding cannot represent" with its migration note (no refusal and no refusal title exist any more); ADR-0029 (as amended: implemented for TXT and Markdown); `design.md` D14 §6; `docs/next_features.md` §6, §10; `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/txt/{TxtWriter,TxtReader}.java` (`encode` throws `CorruptContainerException` today); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/md/{MarkdownWriter,MarkdownReader,ParsedMarkdown}.java` (`targetInner.getBytes(parsed.charset())` writes `?`); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/detect/{CharsetLadder,ForeignWordCoherence}.java` (how a re-open resolves the charset; `ForeignWordCoherence.prefer` never overrides a multi-byte winner); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/fb2/Fb2Writer.java` (`serialize`, the switch this follows); `modules/document/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/document/DocumentService.java` (`write`); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/txt/TxtRoundTripTest.java` (`write_unrepresentableTargetCharacter_failsAndWritesNoFile`, which this task rewrites); `modules/document/src/test/java/ua/bookloom/document/golden/FormatGoldenRoundTripTest.java` (the FB2 encoding-switch case asserted against a re-parsed canonical tree — the carve-out this extends).
  - **Change:** `TxtWriter` and `MarkdownWriter` first encode the assembled output in the charset resolved at import with a `CharsetEncoder` set to `REPORT`; when any character is unrepresentable they decode the untouched source bytes with that charset, splice the targets into the decoded text, and encode the **whole** document as UTF-8, so no byte of the old code page survives; they write a UTF-8 byte-order mark exactly when the source had a mark (its presence, not its bytes) and set the registry's recorded charset (`ParsedMarkdown`, the TXT registry state) to UTF-8. Markdown never writes `?` again. `TxtWriter` no longer throws `CorruptContainerException` for an unrepresentable character, so `DocumentService.write` refuses no export for its encoding. No new exception type, no new error title.
  - **Test first:** `TxtRoundTripTest`: the rewritten case — a TXT read as `windows-1251`, with no byte-order mark, holding `Привіт.\n\nHello.\n`, written with the target `車.` for its second segment → the output decodes as UTF-8 to `Привіт.\n\n車.\n`, begins with no byte-order mark, holds no `?`, and re-opening it resolves `UTF-8`; `windows-1252` `Hello.\n` with the target `Привіт.` → the export succeeds and decodes as UTF-8 to `Привіт.\n`; `windows-1251` `One.\n\nTwo.\n` with the target `Два — три.` for its second segment → the output decodes as `windows-1251` to `One.\n\nДва — три.\n` and the bytes before the second paragraph are unchanged. `MarkdownRoundTripTest`: a Markdown file read as `ISO-8859-1` whose one segment's target contains `Ÿ` → the whole output decodes as UTF-8, contains `Ÿ`, and holds no `?` in its place. `FormatGoldenRoundTripTest`: an encoding-switched TXT fixture and an encoding-switched Markdown fixture are each asserted against the expected text re-parsed from the written file (TXT by decoded text, Markdown re-parse-equal) instead of the source's bytes; the zero-edit goldens are unchanged.
  - **Log:** INFO once per switched document (document id, format, source charset, `UTF-8`, whether a mark was written); DEBUG the reporting encoder's outcome; never the character or the text above TRACE.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew :document:check` are green.

- [x] 6.3 Produce the EPUB navigation document's link texts, the NCX labels that differ from them and every image's `alt` value as auxiliary segments, and write them back — carving the navigation document and the NCX out of "out-of-spine resources are verbatim" while keeping their structure, order, `playOrder` and link targets; writing an NCX label that reads exactly like a navigation label from that navigation segment's target; producing no navigation label for a navigation document that is itself in the spine; and giving an image inside a paragraph its translated `alt` whether or not the paragraph was translated, encoded exactly once. One surveyed book kept 494 table-of-contents labels in English beside translated chapter headings (`docs/next_features.md` §8); translating an identical NCX label a second time wastes a model call and lets a reviewer's edit reach only one of the two; and restoring a paragraph that holds an image would otherwise put the untranslated description back, or print `&amp;amp;` (DD-47, ADR-0041). → `:document`

  - **Change:** the reader reuses task 4.4's `NavigationParser`. When the navigation document is **not** in the spine, each nav link's content becomes a `NAV_LABEL` markup slot `aux:nav:<entry path>` (e.g. `aux:nav:1.3.2`) anchored into the navigation tree; each NCX `navPoint`'s `navLabel/text` becomes a `NAV_LABEL` text slot `aux:ncx:<navPoint id>` only when its source text differs from every navigation-label segment's, and otherwise the slot table records it as written from that navigation segment. When the navigation document is in the spine it yields no `NAV_LABEL` (its links are body segments) and every NCX label is its own segment. Each non-empty `img@alt` in a spine document becomes an `ALT` attribute slot `aux:alt:<unit href>:<node path>` (`AttributeAnchor`). `DocumentService.unmask` restores an `ALT` segment to plain text — every placeholder substituted back, no markup escaping. The writer re-serializes the navigation document when one of its labels has a target or its `html`/`body` language attribute equals the source language (task 4.6's rule), and the NCX when one of its labels has a target, each following task 5.1's line-end and declaration rule and, for the navigation document, task 5.3's prolog rule; otherwise it copies their bytes. An NCX label recorded as written from a navigation segment takes that segment's effective target, so a reviewer's edit reaches both. `ALT` write-back: when the body run that holds the image has a target, the writer finds the image's exact source fragment inside that run's restored markup — the k-th occurrence of identical markup matching the k-th image — and substitutes the same fragment with the translated alt, attribute-encoded, before the run is written, and does not also set the tree attribute (the run's re-parse would overwrite it); when the run has no target, or the image is in no run, the writer sets the attribute on the tree through `TreeNode.setAttribute` as plain text.
  - **Test first:** `EpubReaderTest`: a navigation document outside the spine with 11 entries, an NCX whose 11 labels read exactly like them and 7 images with non-empty `alt` → 11 `NAV_LABEL` and 7 `ALT` segments; a navigation document labelling an entry `Chapter 1` and an NCX labelling it `Chapter I` → two `NAV_LABEL` segments; `nav.xhtml` in the spine with 5 entries → no `NAV_LABEL`, and the 5 link texts are segments of the `nav.xhtml` body unit; `OEBPS/toc01.html` outside the spine with 3 entries → 3 labels with ids `aux:nav:1`, `aux:nav:2`, `aux:nav:3`. `EpubWriterTest`: an NCX `navPoint playOrder="4"` labelled `Chapter Three` pointing at `c03.xhtml#start`, with no navigation document, and the target `Розділ третій` keeps `playOrder="4"` and the link and reads `Розділ третій`; a navigation document and an NCX both labelling a chapter `The Storm`, with the one navigation segment targeted `Буря` → both read `Буря`, and with that segment's saved edit `Шторм` → both read `Шторм`; `<p>Before <img src="fig1.png" alt="Figure 1"/> after</p>` with the body target `До ⟦g0⟧ після` and the alt target `Рисунок 1` → `<p>До <img src="fig1.png" alt="Рисунок 1" /> після</p>`; with the body target `⟦g0⟧ До після` (the image moved) → the image still reads `alt="Рисунок 1"`; with no body target → `<p>Before <img src="fig1.png" alt="Рисунок 1" /> after</p>`; `<p><img src="dot.png" alt="Dot"/> and <img src="dot.png" alt="Dot"/></p>` with the body target `⟦g0⟧ і ⟦g1⟧` and the alt targets `Перша точка` and `Друга точка` → the first image carries `Перша точка` and the second `Друга точка`; the alt target `Том & Джеррі`, inside a translated and inside an untranslated paragraph → `alt="Том &amp; Джеррі"` and no `&amp;amp;` anywhere; `<a href="#n1" title="Footnote one">1</a>` fully translated keeps `href="#n1"` and `title="Footnote one"`; a navigation document whose `<html>` carries `xml:lang="en"`, exported from `en` to `uk` → `xml:lang="uk"`, its prolog kept byte for byte; a rewritten NCX that had no declaration and only LF gains neither. Golden suite green (zero edits: the navigation document and the NCX are copied byte for byte unless their root language follows, which the canonical comparator's language allowance covers).

Keys are field names a program reads, and dates, flags, counts and addresses are data a site generator parses, so translating any of them breaks the file (owner decision D-2); a translation such as `Книга: Частина 1` or `1984` written unquoted becomes a mapping or a number to a YAML reader; a file that still says `lang: en` after translation claims to be English; and eleven images in one surveyed Markdown document kept their English alt text (`docs/next_features.md` §8). → `:document`

  - **Change:** `Frontmatter` records, for each top-level line, the key, the value's byte span (inside its quotes when quoted, a trailing `# comment` excluded when unquoted) and its quote style, and classifies the value as text only when all hold: the key is neither `lang` nor `language`; it is a single-line scalar on a top-level `key: value` line (not indented, not a list item, not a flow collection opening `[` or `{`, not a block scalar opening `|` or `>`, not an anchor, alias or tag opening `&`, `*` or `!`); unquoted, it is not a null (`~`, `null` in any case, empty), a boolean (`true`, `false`, `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off`, `y`, `n` in any case), a number (hexadecimal, octal, `.inf` and `.nan` included) or an ISO date or date-time; it is not a URL or e-mail (`scheme://`, `www.`, `mailto:`, `name@host.domain`); and it holds a letter. `MarkdownReader` produces, only when the file opens with `---`, a `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` segment `aux:fm:<key>` per text value, whose source is the value inside its quotes with `\"` or `''` decoded, and an `ALT` segment `aux:alt:<file name>:img<k>` per image's alt span with its backslash escapes decoded — both `ByteSpanAnchor`s. `DocumentService.unmask` restores `FRONTMATTER_VALUE` and `ALT` to plain text: every placeholder substituted back, no Markdown escaping, no structure check. `MarkdownWriter` writes a value back inside its original quotes (`"` written `\"` inside double quotes, `'` written `''` inside single quotes), and wraps an unquoted translation in double quotes when it contains `: ` or ` #`, starts with one of `-`, `?`, `:`, `,`, `[`, `]`, `{`, `}`, `#`, `&`, `*`, `!`, `|`, `>`, `'`, `"`, `%`, `@` or a backtick, or would itself read as a null, boolean, number or date; it replaces an existing top-level `lang` value with the target tag in that value's quote style, changes nothing else in the frontmatter, never adds a `lang` key and leaves `language` alone; an alt target is written with `\`, `[` and `]` escaped — inside the paragraph's restored image token when the paragraph also has a body target (the spans would overlap), otherwise as its own replacement.
  - **Test first:** `MarkdownRoundTripTest`, with the existing tests rewritten to the new rules: `MarkdownFixtures.PRIMARY` (frontmatter `title: The Book`, `lang: en`) yields exactly one auxiliary segment, `aux:fm:title` holding `The Book`, and none holding `title`, `lang` or `en`; written with zero edits and target `uk`, its output equals the source except that `lang: en` reads `lang: uk`; `---\ntitle: The Book\nlang: en\ndate: 2024-05-01\ndraft: true\n---\n` → exactly one segment, `The Book`; the lines `tags: [war, peace]`, `homepage: https://example.com`, `pages: 1225`, `published: yes`, `summary: |` and `  A long story.` → no segment; `subtitle: "Part 2"` and `edition: "2"` → a segment `Part 2` and none holding `2` alone; `title: The Book # working title` → the segment holds `The Book`; a file beginning `# Title` with a later `---` line → no auxiliary segment and two body segments; the target `Книга` for `title: The Book` → output begins `---\ntitle: Книга\n---\n`; `Книга: Частина 1` → `title: "Книга: Частина 1"`; `1984` for `title: Nineteen Eighty-Four` → `title: "1984"`; `Книга "Перша"` for `title: "The Book"` → `title: "Книга \"Перша\""`; `Книга 'Перша'` for `title: 'The Book'` → `title: 'Книга ''Перша'''`; `Том & Джеррі *назавжди*` → `title: Том & Джеррі *назавжди*` with no backslash; `---\ntitle: The Book\n---\n` exported to `uk` → unchanged; `lang: "en-US"` → `lang: "uk"`; `language: en` with no `lang` → unchanged and no `lang` key added; `![Figure 1](fig1.png)` with the alt target `Рисунок 1` → `![Рисунок 1](fig1.png)`, with and without a body target on its paragraph; `Рисунок [1]` → `![Рисунок \[1\]](fig1.png)`; the source alt `Figure \[1\]` is read as `Figure [1]`. `FormatGoldenRoundTripTest` (Markdown) stays re-parse-equal.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the frontmatter keys seen (names are not book text), how many values were taken as text and why each other was not (the rule, not the value), the alt count, each quoting decision and whether a `lang` value was replaced; TRACE the values.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :document:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.document.golden.*'` and `./gradlew build` are green.

## 8. Quality gates — after group 7

- [x] 8.1 Add the five deterministic soft checks — target script, untranslated echo, repetition loop, length ratio against the pair's band, and glossary compliance over locked terms — each yielding a pass or fail and a margin between 0 and 1, marking a failure as blocking except a failed echo below the 20-code-point floor, with names kept by policy left out of the script and echo checks and both checks skipped for a foreign passage kept by policy. Without them a restored reply is accepted on its tokens alone, so a copied source, a Latin-letter "Ukrainian" answer or a decode loop goes straight into the book; and under owner decision D-3 a check that fails outright must block acceptance, while a short line such as `OK` or a name may read the same in both languages. → `:pipeline`

  - **Excludes:** the refusal gate, the confidence blend and the evaluator that reports a blocking failure — task 8.2; the placeholder and protected-span hard gates — tasks 5.8 and 9.6; feeding the segment's locked terms into the glossary check, and the restored target it must then read — task 9.6 (here the check takes a plain list and reads the target display text); where the checks run in the pipeline — task 8.6.
  - **Read:** `specs/quality-gates/spec.md` "Check the target script for pairs whose languages use different scripts", "Fail a target that echoes its source", "Fail a target caught in a repetition loop", "Check the length ratio against the band of the language pair", "Measure glossary compliance over the locked terms in the segment", "Skip the echo and script checks for a marked foreign passage kept by policy", "Block acceptance when a soft check fails outright" (every scenario of each); design D8 (display texts, the soft-check table with each margin, the names removed under the keep-original policy, the echo floor, foreign keep), "Tuning constants" (the check rows); ADR-0037; `docs/specification/02_Architecture/05_PIPELINE_ENGINE.md#qa-checks`, `#qa-thresholds`, `#confidence` (the margins, which task 0.3 records there); `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/qa/LengthBand.java` and `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/DisplayText.java` (task 7.3).
  - **Change:** in package `ua.bookloom.pipeline.qa` (created by task 7.3): `CheckName` enum carrying each soft check's confidence weight as a constructor field (GLOSSARY 0.30, LENGTH 0.25, SCRIPT 0.20, ECHO 0.15, REPETITION 0.10; the hard gates REFUSAL, PLACEHOLDER, LOCKED_TERM and KEPT_RUN with none — the last two being the two outcomes of task 9.6's one protected-span gate) and the finding type it raises (SCRIPT and ECHO `language`, REPETITION `fluency`, LENGTH `omission`, GLOSSARY `glossary`, REFUSAL `meaning`, PLACEHOLDER `markup`, LOCKED_TERM `glossary`, KEPT_RUN `markup`); `CheckResult` record (`CheckName`, `margin`, `passed`, `skipped`, `blocking`, `@Nullable QaFinding`) — a failed soft check is `blocking` with a `medium` finding, except a failed echo whose source display text, after the name removal below, has fewer than 20 code points, which is not blocking and carries a `low` `language` finding; `TextSimilarity` (NFC via `java.text.Normalizer`, `toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`, `1 − Levenshtein / max length`); `ScriptCheck`, `EchoCheck` (owning the echo floor), `RepetitionCheck`, `LengthCheck` (reading `LengthBand`) and `GlossaryCheck`, run in that fixed order by `SoftChecks.run(SoftCheckInput)`; `SoftCheckInput` carrying the source and target display texts (`DisplayText.of` over the masked texts, so every document and protected token is already gone), the brief's source language (`@Nullable`) and the target language, the `ForeignPassagePolicy`, the `NamePolicy`, the segment's `@Nullable` declared block language, the glossary's terms (for the keep-original removal) and a `List<LockedRendering(term, rendering)>` of the locked terms present in the segment. Pass, fail and margins as design D8 fixes them (task 0.3 states the same in `05_PIPELINE_ENGINE.md#confidence`): a failed check 0.0, a skipped one 1.0; script — skipped when the source display text has fewer than 20 code points, the two languages share a script or the target has no letters, fails a share below 0.60 of the target's letters in the target language's script, margin `clamp((share − 0.60) / 0.20)`; echo — fails at a similarity of 0.90 or more, margin `clamp((0.90 − similarity) / 0.10)`; repetition — fails a sequence of 3 words repeated 3 or more times in a row, margin 0.5 for exactly two in a row, else 1.0; length — fails outside the pair's band (`LengthBand.forPair(…).widenedFor(sourceChars)`), margin `clamp(distance to the nearer bound / (0.10 × band width))`; glossary — skipped with no locked term present, fails when any present locked term's rendering is missing from the target, else 1.0. Under `NamePolicy.KEEP_ORIGINAL` every whole-word occurrence of a glossary term is removed from both display texts before the script share, the echo similarity and the echo floor are computed. A segment is marked foreign only under `ForeignPassagePolicy.KEEP`, when its declared block language, normalized, differs from the brief's normalized source language, or when the dominant script of its source display text differs from the source language's; a marked segment skips echo and script; under any other policy nothing is marked. The name removal under `KEEP_ORIGINAL` comes before every skip rule, so a names-only target has no letters left and skips the script check; an empty source display text (a segment that is all tokens) skips echo and length; an unknown source language marks nothing foreign and leaves the script check running; an uncatalogued target language skips the script check. Every finding a check raises names the check in `QaFinding.raisedBy`, a component added to the `:api` record (`specs/quality-gates/spec.md` "Record each segment's findings for review and repair" asks for the check or judge that raised it), carried by its `CheckName` as a wire string: `script`, `echo`, `repetition`, `length`, `glossary`, `refusal`, `placeholder`, `locked-term`, `kept-run` (the judge's findings say `judge`). The script lookup `LanguageTags.normalize(tag).flatMap(Languages::byTag).map(Language::script)`, written privately in `LengthBand` and `TokenEstimator`, becomes `util.lang.Languages.scriptOf`, which all three use.

en→pl `Otworzył stare drzwi.` and the Czech `Otevřel staré dveře.` skip 1.0; `Yes, sir.` (9 code points) skips 1.0; under `KEEP_ORIGINAL` with the glossary `Hale`, `Margaret`, `Milton`, `London`, the target `Hale і Margaret Hale виїхали з Milton до London рано того холодного ранку.` for `Hale and Margaret Hale left Milton for London early that cold morning.` has share 1.0 and passes, and under `TRANSLITERATE` the same target's share is 0.54 and fails; echo: the verbatim copy and `HE OPENED THE OLD DOOR.` fail 0.0, blocking with a medium `language` finding; `It was a dark night.` → `IT WAS A DARK NIGHT.` (exactly 20 code points) fails blocking; `Yes, sir.` → `YES, SIR.` fails 0.0, not blocking, with a low `language` finding; the Ukrainian target passes 1.0; under `KEEP_ORIGINAL` with the glossary `Margaret Hale`, `Milton Northern`, the unchanged `Margaret Hale, Milton Northern.` fails the echo without blocking; repetition: `двері відчинилися знову` three times in a row fails 0.0 (blocking, medium `fluency`), `двері відчинилися знову двері відчинилися знову і він увійшов` passes 0.5; length en→uk 100→50 characters fails (blocking, medium `omission`), 100→115 passes 1.0, 200→151 (ratio 0.755) passes 0.5, `Go.`→`Іди-но звідси.` fails in the widened band 0.35–3.6 and `Go.`→`Йди.` passes, uk→el ratio 2.3 passes in the default band 0.5–2.5; glossary: `Hale`→`Гейл` and `Milton`→`Мілтон` both rendered 1.0, only one of the two rendered fails 0.0 with a medium `glossary` finding, no locked term skipped 1.0, the unlocked `Baker Street` rendered `вулиця Бейкер` 1.0; foreign: KEEP with block language `fr` and `Je ne regrette rien.` returned unchanged skips echo and script, KEEP and `Γνῶθι σεαυτόν` unchanged in an English book skips both, KEEP and the unmarked `He opened the old door.` unchanged fails echo, KEEP with block language `en-GB` and source `en` is not marked, KEEP with source `en` and no block language is not marked even for a book whose package declares `uk` (the input carries only the brief's source), TRANSLATE with block `fr` unchanged fails echo; under `KEEP_ORIGINAL` with the glossary `Margaret Hale`, `Milton Northern`, the unchanged `Margaret Hale, Milton Northern.` skips script at 1.0; a source of only `⟦g0⟧⟦g1⟧` skips echo and length; an unknown source language marks nothing; a target language outside the catalogue skips script; the length margin of 200→151 is asserted within 1e-9 (the double sum is 0.5000000000000003) and the uk→el row uses a source of at least 25 characters so its band is not widened. With the judge on, `start` makes one judge call over the `Drafted` outcomes whose hard gates passed — never a `FlaggedAtOnce` one, and no call when none qualifies — labelled `s1…sk` in document order. Decisions: `FlaggedAtOnce` → FLAGGED with its error and no machine target; `Drafted` → the acceptance rule, else up to `repairRounds` self-heal rounds: a directed fix when the segment has a concrete finding (a failed hard gate — a `markup` or protected-span finding supplies the expected token sequence —, a soft check failed outright, or a medium or high judge finding), otherwise reflect → improve (confidence below τ, a judge score below τ_judge with no finding, or an unreadable verdict), then polish only when `Borderline.isBorderline` holds for the improved target; each round's target goes through the gate and `QaEvaluator`; with the judge on, a round's target that passes its hard gates, fails no check and reaches τ is judged again alone (label `s1`) and decided by that call's score and findings instead of the chunk's, while a target short of any of these goes to the next round with no judge call; a self-heal reply that is empty or cut off, or a model `emptyCompletion` or `contextWindow`, FLAGS the segment at once keeping the last target that passed every hard gate as its machine target, while a self-heal reply that is not the required object or fails the placeholder gate fails that round (a self-heal call gets no format repair); still failing after the last round → FLAGGED with every finding; accepted after at least one round → path REPAIRED; `machineTarget` and `maskedMachineTarget` are always the last target that passed every hard gate, or none. A self-heal call answers task 8.4's `RepairReply`: `Rewritten` goes through the gate and the checks, `Malformed` fails the round, `FlagNow` flags at once; a judge call answered `emptyCompletion` or `contextWindow` reads as an unreadable verdict, and any other model error ends the step. A round rewrites the latest target that passed the placeholder gate, or else the rejected reply; polish belongs to the round of its improve. A FLAGGED segment records the findings of its last evaluation plus those of the verdict that last decided it, without duplicates, the confidence of its last evaluated target, and the score of that verdict (the chunk's or the re-judge's). A segment drafted in pieces is repaired like any other here; re-running its pieces with the findings as an extra instruction belongs to task 10.8. When polish answers `FlagNow`, `Malformed` or a target that fails a hard gate, the round keeps the improved target, which is the machine target and the last evaluation a flag records. The verdict a FLAGGED segment records is the chunk's until a re-judge replaces it, even after rounds that were not judged. `SegmentPath` DRAFT means no self-heal round ran and REPAIRED that at least one did, for an ACCEPTED and a FLAGGED segment alike (the `:api` Javadoc says so). `QualityCallsLiveTest` (`liveLocal`, env-gated, both dialects) sends the judge, a directed fix, reflect and improve to a real local model and checks only their structure.
