The result is ranked by count descending, then first occurrence, each with the first sentence holding it. There is no `propose` method: `newTerms` uses the threshold 3 as a named constant, and a lone capital letter (the pronoun `I`) is never a single-word candidate. A known limit, kept on purpose: when a sentence opens with a multi-word name (`Baker Street was quiet.`) only its first word is skipped, so its tail (`Street`) can reach the threshold from such openings; skipping the whole run would instead lose every name that follows a sentence opener (`Then Hale nodded.`), which is far more common, and proposals are unlocked and removable. `newTerms(String projectId, List<Segment>, GlossaryRepository)` → `Result<List<GlossaryEntry>>` reads the glossary once with `all`, skips a term the glossary holds ignoring case or `wasRemoved` returns true for, and builds entries with `GlossaryIds`, type other, gender unknown, unlocked, target null; it writes nothing.

`remove` — `GlossaryRepository.remove`. `glossary.GlossaryCsv`: a hand-written RFC-4180 reader and writer (UTF-8; a field quoted when it holds a comma, a quote, CR or LF; quotes doubled; type and gender written as the lower-case constant names; `locked` as `true` or `false`); a row is malformed when it lacks five fields or holds an unknown type, gender or `locked` value; import matches an existing entry by term ignoring case and updates it, adds the others, and reports line numbers (the header is line 1; a quoted field spanning lines reports the line its record starts on).
  - **Test first:** `GlossaryServiceImplTest` (injector of `DocumentModule` and `PersistenceModule`): adding `Justine` → `Жустіна`, character, female, locked lists it with those values; adding `justine` → `Юстина` answers `validation` naming `justine` and adds nothing; adding `Justine` locked with no target answers `validation` with `A locked term needs a target.`; locking `Hale` with an empty target is refused with that message and `Hale` stays unlocked; clearing locked `Hale` → `Гейл`'s target is refused and the target stays `Гейл`; setting `Hale`'s target to `Гейл` and locking it reads back target `Гейл`, locked; changing locked `Hale` from `Хейл` to `Гейл` records one `TERM` deferral on a decided segment reading `Хейл пішов.`; the scan runs on an empty glossary and adds nothing on one holding `Hale` → `Гейл`; after the person removes `Chapter`, a scan of the now-empty glossary over a book mentioning it five times mid-sentence does not propose it, and adding `Chapter` afterwards succeeds; `prescan` on a brief with no target language answers `validation` and makes no call; listing and scanning make no model call. `GlossaryCsvTest` (files in `@TempDir`): `Hale, Margaret` → `Гейл, Маргарет`, character, female, locked exports as `"Hale, Margaret","Гейл, Маргарет",character,female,true`; a file with the header, line 2 `Hale,Гейл,character,male,true`, line 3 `Milton,Мілтон` and a valid line 4 reports `GlossaryImportReport(2, [3], [])`; line 2 `Hale,,character,male,true` reports `(0, [], [2])` and adds no `Hale`; importing `Hale,Гейл,character,male,true` over `Hale` → `Хейл` leaves one `Hale` entry with target `Гейл`, locked; an exported glossary imports back into an empty one unchanged.
  - **Log:** INFO an import or export: path, entries written or imported, malformed and refused counts; DEBUG add, update, remove and scan with the term and the outcome; WARN a refused duplicate, a refused locked entry with no target, and each malformed or refused line number.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.pipeline.glossary.*'` is green and `./gradlew build` is green (an `:api` shape changed).

## 10. The run over the stored project — after group 9 (10.1 and 10.2 are independent of each other; failure routing, 10.4, and the run's seams, 10.5, land before the chunk lifecycle, 10.6, which needs them)

- [x] 10.1 Add an output cap to the chat request and send it to both providers — `ChatRequest.maxOutputTokens`, sent as Ollama `options.num_predict` and as OpenAI-compatible `max_tokens` only when it is set, and computed by `:pipeline` for every call that states an expected output as `max(64, ⌈1.5 × allowance⌉ + 16 + 6 × placeholder tokens)`. A small model that loops on one sentence otherwise writes until the three-minute timeout, three attempts in a row (the checkpoint's `qwen2.5:1.5b` run took about nine minutes for one paragraph); a cap turns the runaway into a cut-off reply, which the reply rules already flag at once (design D3 rule 3, D6) → `:api`, `:llm`, `:pipeline`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 1.5 (`ChatRequest`'s context and expected-output fields), 3.1–3.3 (both clients' request shaping and the timeout), 7.3 (`TokenEstimator`, `ChatRequests`, `DisplayText`), 8.4–8.5 (the self-heal calls that build requests), 9.2 (`Tokens`, which reads the ordered `⟦gN⟧` tokens of a text).
  - **Excludes:** the export writer — task 10.2, which runs alongside this one (∥; no file is shared); a cap on the judge, reflect, pre-scan or summary call (they state no expected output and stay uncapped); the expected-output timeout (unchanged — `expectedOutputTokens` keeps scaling it); a configurable margin; measuring how often the cap cuts a real reply — the hand run, task 16.3.
  - **Read:** `specs/llm-provider/spec.md` "Cap a chat call's output where the request states a cap" (all three scenarios), "Shape an Ollama-native chat request" ("An output cap joins the options"); `specs/inference/spec.md` "Carry a temperature and a response format per call" ("The pseudo model ignores both"); `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Cap the output of every call that states an expected output" (all three scenarios), "Give every call of a run its context size and expected output"; design D4 (the output allowance), D6, "Tuning constants"; `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/llm/ChatRequest.java`; `modules/llm/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/llm/dto/{OllamaChatRequest,OpenAiChatRequest}.java`, `.../llm/client/ollama/OllamaClient.java` (`serializeRequest` `:174–186`, the "Ollama chat started" line `:98–105`), `.../llm/client/openai/OpenAiCompatibleClient.java` (396 lines; `parseFormat`…`toOpenAiMessage` `:151–197`, `ParsedFormat` `:395`, its chat-start line `:102–109`), `.../llm/GatedChatModel.java` (`:122,130` use the copy methods); `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/chunk/TokenEstimator.java`, `.../prompt/ChatRequests.java`, `.../SegmentTranslator.java` (`requestFor`), `.../PieceDrafter.java`, `.../heal/{SelfHealCalls,DirectedFix,Polish,ReflectImprove}.java`, `.../judge/JudgeCall.java`.
  - **Change:** `:api` — `ChatRequest` gains a nullable `maxOutputTokens` (positive when set) as its seventh component; the one-, three- and four-argument constructors and a new six-argument one (today's canonical shape, so existing callers compile) delegate with `null`; `withoutResponseFormat()` and `withoutReasoning()` keep the cap, so a capability downgrade retried by `GatedChatModel` still sends it. `:llm` — `OllamaChatRequest.Options(temperature, numCtx, numPredict)` (`:64`, JSON `num_predict`) and its constructor's null-collapse (`:34`) needs all three null; `OllamaClient.serializeRequest` builds `Options` when any of temperature, context window or cap is set and its chat-start line names the cap. First extract `client/openai/OpenAiRequestMapper` from `OpenAiCompatibleClient` (the four request-shaping methods and `ParsedFormat`, unchanged, so the client falls well under 400 lines); then `OpenAiChatRequest` gains `@JsonProperty("max_tokens") @Nullable Integer maxTokens` (the record is already NON_NULL), which the mapper fills, and the client's chat-start line names the cap. `:pipeline` — `chunk.TokenEstimator.outputCap(allowance, tokenCount)` is the formula (ceiling of 1.5 × allowance); new `prompt.OutputLimit(expectedTokens, capTokens)` with `OutputLimit.forSource(maskedSource, sourceTag, targetTag)` answering `null` for an empty display text and otherwise expected = `TokenEstimator.outputAllowance(DisplayText.of(maskedSource), …)` and cap = `outputCap(allowance, <the number of ⟦gN⟧ tokens in maskedSource>)`; `ChatRequests.build(name, messages, @Nullable OutputLimit limit, lowerTemperature)` replaces its `Integer` parameter and sets both fields. - [x] 10.3 Migrate the job's contract to a run over a stored project, changing no translation decision: `RunRequest` in place of `TranslationRequest`; `JobStage` `PREP`/`TRANSLATE`/`REVISE` with no export stage; `JobReport` without `written` and counting the whole project; each decision stored through `CheckpointPort` as it is made; the run starting at the project's first pending segment; and every caller in `:ui` and `:app` migrated minimally, with an interim bridge that still exports after a window run. Review, export, retry and resume all need the run's decisions stored in one place instead of inside the job, a stopped run can resume only if it starts where the stored decisions end, and doing this with today's acceptance keeps every existing count as it is, so the tasks that change what a run decides (10.6 on) are measured against a green baseline (ADR-0034, ADR-0035) → `:api`, `:pipeline`, `:ui`, `:app`

`PausePoint.BETWEEN_STAGES` reads "after translation, before revision or the end". `:pipeline` — new package `ua.bookloom.pipeline.run` (`package-info.java`, `opens … to com.google.guice`). `run.RunStores` (`ProjectRepository`, `SegmentRepository`, `CheckpointPort`, `OpenProjects`) is built by `TranslationEngineImpl` from its injected ports and handed to the job with a `Clock`, which `PipelineModule` now provides (`Clock.systemUTC()`; none exists today); `newJob` only null-checks. `run.RunStart` refuses, as `ErrorCode.validation` before any model call, a brief target (null included) or source language failing `^[A-Za-z]{2,3}(-[A-Za-z0-9]{2,8})*$` (the engine's pattern, moved), an unknown project, and a project whose book is no longer open; a second `run()` stays `TranslationJobErrors.alreadyRun()`. `run.WorkList` (from `OpenProjects.get(projectId)` and `SegmentRepository.all`) replaces `JobProgressTracker` and `SegmentWork`: PENDING records not kept as source, in document order, each with its Segment and zero-based body section, starting at the first pending, with today's interim skip of the auxiliary unit (`new AlsoTranslate(false, false, false, false).keptKinds()`), today's preceding-target rule (the last three accepted targets of the section) and progress counters started from one `countsByStatus` read and moved per decision, so no query runs per decision. `Decision` gains a nullable `maskedTarget` (the gate's `Restored.maskedForm`), and `run.OutcomeRecords` turns a `Decision` into the stored `SegmentRecord` (status, machine target in plain and masked form, confidence, path `DRAFT`); a flag-at-once reason is stored as the finding `QaFinding(kind = <ErrorCode name>, HIGH, <message>, raisedBy "reply")`, and `OutcomeRecords` reads a flagged record's report code back (that finding's kind, else `ErrorCode.validation`). Each decided segment is committed at once as a one-segment `ChunkCommit` before the boundary that follows. `JobReport` counts are the project's, read when the run ends: `segments` = pending + accepted + revised + flagged, `accepted` = accepted + revised, `flagged`, `flaggedSegments` from `SegmentRepository.flagged`. Polish and the reflect/improve/directed-fix rewrites keep the span tokens on purpose, because the protected-span gate requires each token to come back exactly once.

- [x] 10.10 Reuse the translation memory in the run: before drafting, a segment whose context matches a stored entry is checked at once and, when it passes its gates, checks and τ, is accepted without a draft or the judge; every accepted segment writes a memory entry with its commit.   - **Change:** `DraftOutcome` gains the third sealed record `Reused` (segment, masked source, the stored masked target, its restored target); `AcceptanceRule.acceptsReuse(QaResult, double tau)` is the acceptance rule without the judge term; `QualityLoop` keeps a `Reused` outcome out of the chunk's judge call (no judge request when every outcome is reused). `ProtectedSpans.checkRestored(String storedMaskedTarget, ProtectedMask)` → `Result<String>` is added here, its first user: each span's restored text must occur in the stored target as many times as its token occurs in the masked source — whole-word for a locked rendering, verbatim for a kept run — else the same high finding as the gate; passing, it answers the target re-masked. `ChunkRunner`, per segment in the draft phase: `TranslationMemory.lookup(segment, unitSegments)` → `TmLookup(reuse, hints, suggestions)`; a `reuse` is checked at once — `checkRestored`, then the chunk's gate (task 10.9), `QaEvaluator.evaluate` and `acceptsReuse(qa, τ)`; passing, it becomes `Reused`, decided in its turn as ACCEPTED with path `TM_REUSE` and the computed confidence; failing, it is discarded and the segment drafted as usual with the lookup's hints and suggestions in its context package (the `tmLookup` argument task 10.9 left empty). `PendingCommit` gains TM entries: `TranslationMemory.entryFor(segment, unitSegments, maskedTarget)` for every ACCEPTED segment (a reuse's replaces the identical entry); a FLAGGED segment writes none. `MemoryUpdated(TM, <the segment's locator text>)` is sent on each accepted reuse, exactly as design D5 states. The locator text comes from `pipeline.project.SegmentLocators.of(Document) → Map<String, SegmentLocator>`, created here, its first user: `SegmentLocator.of(kind, unitOrdinal, segmentOrdinal)` applied to every segment in document order, the unit ordinal being the unit's position among the body units (1-based; `0` for the auxiliary unit, `Unit.isAuxiliary()`) and the segment ordinal its 1-based position in its unit (an auxiliary segment's among the auxiliary segments of its kind); the run builds the map once from the `Document` `OpenProjects` holds, and tasks 10.13, 11.1, 11.4 and 11.5 name segments through it too.

- [x] 10.11 Refresh the rolling summary, add the names a unit introduces, and record deferrals with each chunk: after every decision the summary keeper counts, at each unit's end it refreshes (on Max with one model call), a deterministic scan over everything decided so far proposes new names into the unit's last commit, and each chunk's judge and unknown-gender deferrals are stored — announcing every glossary and summary update. A run without these forgets who is who from one chapter to the next and leaves backward revision nothing to fix; each part was built alone in group 9 (design D2, D5, D10; owner decision D-5) → `:pipeline`
  - **Carried from group 9 (task 9.10):** first add to `revision.DeferralRegister` (static, pure, writes nothing; id `projectId:segmentId:REASON:waitingOn`) the two producers this task is the first to call, with their tests in `DeferralRegisterTest`: `fromJudge(String projectId, List<JudgeDeferral>)` → `JUDGE` deferrals with `waitingOn` = the judge's reason (a judge deferral for `ch01.xhtml:9` becomes a `JUDGE` deferral on that id whose `waitingOn` is the judge's reason); `unknownGender(String projectId, Segment, List<GlossaryEntry>)` → one `GENDER_UNKNOWN` per character entry of gender `UNKNOWN` whose term occurs whole-word (`WholeWord`) in the segment's display text, `waitingOn` = the term (`ch01.xhtml:9` mentioning `Sam` (character, unknown) → one `GENDER_UNKNOWN` with `waitingOn` `Sam`; `Sam` and `Alex` both unknown → two; `Sam` female → none; a place of unknown gender → none). Log each recorded deferral at DEBUG with segment id, reason and waiting-on. `termChanged` and the storage key with `waitingOn` already exist.
  - **Carried from group 9 (task 9.8):** `RollingSummaryKeeper` puts a unit's whole source and accepted targets into the Max summary prompt with no budget; a very long chapter can exceed the model's context. This task caps the chapter text sent (for example to the model's context budget through `TokenEstimator`, dropping the middle) before it wires the keeper into the run, and states the cap in `design.md` "Tuning constants". The keeper takes the project id from the first accepted `SegmentRecord`; a run with no accepted record has nothing to summarize, so this needs no constructor argument. `RollingSummary.tokensSince` holds a count of accepted segments (its Javadoc says so); its name is a known mismatch and a rename belongs to a separate `:api` change.
  - **Depends on:** tasks 8.6 (`ChunkDecider`, `SegmentHealer`), 9.4 (`FrequencyScan.newTerms`, `GlossaryIds`), 9.8 (`RollingSummaryKeeper`), 9.10 (`DeferralRegister`), 10.10.
  - **Excludes:** a TERM deferral when the person edits a locked term — task 9.11 records it; backward revision on Max — task 11.4; showing these updates in the window — task 12.6.
  - **Read:** `specs/translation-pipeline/spec.md` "Keep a rolling summary of the book so far" (all three scenarios), "Propose new names at the end of each body unit", "Record deferrals and revise backwards on Max" (the recording sentence, "One TERM deferral per changed locked term per segment; JUDGE deferrals are record-only"), "Report progress and the outcome" ("Events for a three-segment book"); `specs/glossary/spec.md` "Keep the person's entries and removals when names are proposed"; design D2 (commits), D5 (`MemoryUpdated`), D10; `modules/pipeline/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/pipeline/heal/{ChunkDecider,SegmentHealer}.java`, `.../run/{ChunkRunner,PendingCommit}.java`.
  - **Change:** `ChunkDecider.deferrals()` returns every judge deferral of the chunk, the ones from re-judges of a repaired segment included (`SegmentHealer` hands each verdict's deferrals up); `ChunkRunner` puts `DeferralRegister.fromJudge(projectId, chunk.deferrals())` and `DeferralRegister.unknownGender(projectId, segment, glossary)` for each decided segment into that chunk's `PendingCommit`. `RollingSummaryKeeper.onDecided(segment, record)` runs after every decision and `onUnitEnd(unitId)` at each unit's end — on Max one `SUMMARY` call through `ModelCalls`; an `AppError` it answers is routed like any model call's (task 10.4), an unreadable reply keeps the previous version — and each `Optional<RollingSummary>` it answers is announced. The summary used by the context package (task 10.9's `ContextInputs.summary`) and by the chunk headroom's estimate is the latest stored version (`SummaryRepository.latest`: the model's `target` when non-empty, else the deterministic `source`). At the end of each **body** unit `ChunkRunner` runs `FrequencyScan.newTerms(projectId, decidedSegments, glossaryRepository)` over every segment decided so far in the book, whatever its status, and puts the proposals into that unit's last commit as glossary additions — the commit's own rule skips any term the person added or removed meanwhile; the re-scan is quadratic over a book and accepted as such. `MemoryUpdated` is sent as design D5 states and by nothing else: `GLOSSARY` labelled `+n` when `PREP`'s scan or a unit-end scan added n ≥ 1 entries (the entries the commit actually added), `SUMMARY` labelled with the new version.

Each part is under 400 lines; the two instance parts are `@Slf4j @RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})` classes that task 11.2 injects. `:api` `ReviewCounts` (`modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/pipeline/ReviewCounts.java`, today `total, autoAccepted, repairedAccepted, flagged, reviewed, pending, sourceKept`) gains an eighth, last component `flaggedWithoutTarget` — the FLAGGED records with no machine target, which stay counted in `flagged` and are written in the source language — rejected when negative or above `flagged`, like its siblings.
    - `review.SegmentActions` — `accept`, `saveEdit`, `revert`, `skip`, `acceptProposal`. Each reads the record with `SegmentRepository.find`, checks the status machine, and writes through `SegmentRepository.update`, which replaces that one record only. `accept`: FLAGGED → ACCEPTED with its machine target; ACCEPTED → ACCEPTED unchanged but reviewed; any other status, or a FLAGGED record with no machine target, → `ErrorCode.validation` (the latter with `There is no machine translation to accept — edit it or retry.`) and nothing changes. `saveEdit(maskedText)` on FLAGGED, ACCEPTED or REVISED: the text is the masked edit as the editor shows it (`maskedUserTarget`, else `maskedMachineTarget`, else the masked source), restored through 9.3's gate alone; success → REVISED, path `USER`, with `userTarget` and `maskedUserTarget` stored and the machine target kept; failure → `validation` naming the broken placeholder, record unchanged. `revert`: REVISED → ACCEPTED with both user forms cleared (path back to REPAIRED when the record has repair rounds, else DRAFT); a REVISED record with no machine target → `validation`, since there is nothing to revert to. `skip`: no change, answers the next FLAGGED record's id (`ReviewDeskImpl.skip`, task 11.2, loads that record for the port's `Result<SegmentRecord>`). `acceptProposal`: reads the segment's open deferral holding a proposal; the proposal becomes `userTarget` and the masked proposal `maskedUserTarget`, status REVISED, path `USER`, reviewed, the deferral resolved; no proposal → `validation`. A move to PENDING, or any transition outside the machine, → `validation`.
    - `review.ReviewQueries` — `queue` and `segment`, each reading the brief's current `alsoTranslate().keptKinds()`. `ALL_FLAGGED` every FLAGGED record not kept as source; `NAMES` those with a `glossary` finding; `OMISSIONS` those with an `omission` finding; `FOREIGN_KEPT` those marked foreign or holding a kept foreign run (`qa.ForeignMarking` gains one public overload over a segment, the source language and the policy; a kept run is a `KEPT_RUN` span of `ProtectedSpans.mask` called with no glossary — no second copy); `ALL_SEGMENTS` every decided record and every record kept as source by choice, those with path `SOURCE_KEPT`. Views are in document order. Masked and display source, declared languages and unit ordinals come from the document `OpenProjects` holds (a project with no open document → `validation`); the locator comes from `pipeline.project.SegmentLocators.of(Document)`, which task 10.10 created — tasks 11.4 and 11.5 name segments through it too.
    - `review.ReviewCounting` — static (`@NoArgsConstructor(access = AccessLevel.PRIVATE)`) `count(List<SegmentRecord>, Set<SegmentKind> keptKinds) → ReviewCounts`: total = every record; sourceKept = records kept as source by choice, counted in no other field; autoAccepted = ACCEPTED, path `DRAFT` or `TM_REUSE`, not reviewed; repairedAccepted = ACCEPTED, path `REPAIRED`, not reviewed; flagged = FLAGGED; flaggedWithoutTarget = FLAGGED with no machine target; reviewed = `reviewed` true; pending = PENDING and not kept. The desk's `counts` (task 11.2) and task 11.5's report both call it.

Only on FLAGGED or ACCEPTED (`validation` otherwise). `ErrorCode.busy` and no call while `RunRepository.latest(projectId)` reads `RUNNING`; allowed on `PAUSED`, `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED`, `FAILED` or no run. It rebuilds the draft from the snapshot's texts alone — preceding targets, terms (the locked ones masked through `ProtectedSpans.mask`, which takes `GlossaryEntry` values, so retry builds one from each `SnapshotTerm` — `new GlossaryEntry(term, projectId, term, target, type, gender, locked)`, the mask reading only term, target and locked — and no overload is added), memory hits, summary, style sheet; no repository lookup. It runs the draft step 10.6 left — `SegmentTranslator` has been public since 10.6, so no copy — with the note as `[Extra instruction]`, at `PromptName.DRAFT.temperature(lowerTemperature)`, through a `ModelCalls` that goes straight to the bound model — `(kind, id, request) -> model.chat(request)`, since no job runs — then 9.3's gate, the quality loop with zero repair rounds (`QualityLoop.start` over `DialParameters.of(brief.dial()).withoutRepairRounds()`: the loop's one `heal.QaEvaluation`, one `JudgeCall` over that pair alone (label `s1`) when the dial enables the judge and the candidate passed the hard gates, and `AcceptanceRule.accepts(qa, verdict, segmentId, mode.threshold())`), so nothing of the loop is copied and no self-heal round runs. A record with no stored snapshot → `validation` ("nothing to replay"); a FLAGGED retry whose reply never passed the hard gates keeps the earlier machine target (a machine target is never lost); a retry's judge deferrals are not stored. `SegmentTranslator` gains one public overload taking the note and the lower-temperature flag, `ContextPackageAssembler.replay(ContextSnapshot, ProtectedMask)` rebuilds the draft's context from the snapshot alone, and `RetryDraft` and `ReviewDeskImpl` are bound in task 11.6 with `ReviewMode`. Pass → ACCEPTED, path `DRAFT`, the new machine target in both forms, reviewed. Failure on FLAGGED → stays FLAGGED with the new findings, not reviewed. Failure on ACCEPTED → the stored record is untouched and the answer is that record `withFindings` the new ones (never a downgrade). The snapshot is kept, so every retry replays what the first draft saw. The stored record is built by 10.5's `OutcomeRecords`, so its findings carry `raisedBy` as the run's do. `ReviewDeskImpl implements ReviewDesk` (`@Slf4j @RequiredArgsConstructor(onConstructor_ = {@Inject})` over `SegmentActions`, `ReviewQueries`, `RetryDraft` and the record repository `counts` reads) keeps only the boundary `try/catch` — any `Throwable` becomes `Result.err(AppError(internal, …, cause))`, logged once with its cause — the log lines and delegation: `accept`, `saveEdit`, `revert`, `skip`, `acceptProposal` and `retry` to the parts, `queue` and `segment` to `ReviewQueries`, `counts` to `ReviewCounting.count` over the project's records with the brief's `alsoTranslate().keptKinds()`. It holds no status logic of its own.
  - **Test first:** `RetryDraftTest` (real `DocumentModule`, the `PersistenceModule` repositories, `ScriptedChatModel`; segments addressed by id): latest run `PAUSED`, Balanced, `ch05.xhtml:11` (FLAGGED, source `The monster met me at midnight.`) retried with note `keep it more formal` and a lower temperature → one draft request at `0.1` whose user message holds the note under `[Extra instruction]`, the snapshot's preceding target and `Hale` → `Хейл` although the glossary now reads `Гейл`, then one judge request labelled `s1`; replies `Чудовисько зустріло мене опівночі.` and `{"score":0.9,"verdict":"accept"}` → ACCEPTED, path `DRAFT`, reviewed; run `CANCELLED` → the draft request is made; run `RUNNING` → `ErrorCode.busy`, no request; a retried FLAGGED target of 14 characters for the 31-character source (ratio 0.45 against the 0.81–1.69 band) → stays FLAGGED with a new `medium` `omission` finding raised by `length`, not reviewed, no directed-fix request; the same on ACCEPTED `ch02.xhtml:3` → stored record unchanged, answer carrying the finding; retry of a PENDING or a REVISED segment → `validation`. `ReviewDeskImplTest` (the same real collaborators, the real parts): a `@ParameterizedTest` over the six actions (Accept, Save edit, Revert, Retry, Skip, Apply proposal) — none ever leaves a segment PENDING; one port-level test per action proving the desk delegates to its part and answers its `Result` — `accept` on FLAGGED `ch05.xhtml:11` → ACCEPTED, `saveEdit` → REVISED, `revert` → ACCEPTED, `skip` → the next FLAGGED id, `acceptProposal` on `ch02.xhtml:6` with a stored proposal → REVISED with that text, `retry` on a `RUNNING` run → `busy` — and one each for `queue(ALL_FLAGGED)`, `segment` and `counts` answering what the parts answer (a project of 3 accepted and 1 flagged record → `counts` total 4, flagged 1); the boundary: an unknown segment id on each action → `ErrorCode.validation` and no thrown exception, and a repository fake that throws → `ErrorCode.internal` with the cause kept and one ERROR log line. Unit tier; the Retry cases keep their `method_state_expected` names.

(8) `:api` `SideFile` gains a constructor-injected suffix field (`.glossary.csv`, `.bilingual.html`, `.report.md`) and `Path pathBeside(Path destination, BookFormat format)` — the destination's file name minus its format suffix (`format.matchedSuffix(fileName)`, so `Kobzar.uk.fb2.zip` gives `Kobzar.uk`, never `Kobzar.uk.fb2`), plus the side file's suffix, in the destination's folder; a file name the format does not match keeps its whole name and no exception is thrown. It is the one naming rule: step (1)'s occupied-path check and `export.SideFiles` call it, and task 15.1's `:ui` calls it too, because `:ui` cannot see `:pipeline`'s internal packages. `export.SideFiles`, named through it: `<name>.glossary.csv` through `GlossaryCsv` (`GLOSSARY_CSV`), `<name>.bilingual.html` (`BILINGUAL_HTML`: one `<tr>` per segment, display source | written target, HTML-escaped, inline CSS, no URL, script or external reference), `<name>.report.md` (`QUALITY_REPORT`: counts, flagged segments by locator with findings, the pass's notes) — the contents are built in memory before the move so a failed build writes nothing, and each file lands through a temporary and a move; a side-file I/O failure after the move answers `ErrorCode.internal` naming the file and leaves the verified book. (9) `ExportReport`: `written` = ACCEPTED, REVISED and FLAGGED-with-machine-target records; `pending` = PENDING records of translated kinds **and FLAGGED records with no machine target**, all written in the source language; `sourceKept`, `flaggedWritten`, `autoAccepted` and `reviewed` from `ReviewCounting`; `verifiedSegments` = body segments. Any failure removes the temporary file, writes no side file and leaves the destination unchanged; `cancel()` takes effect before the move and removes the temporary file. The export never reads or changes a run's control state.
  - **Test first:** `ExportJobImplTest` and its siblings (`ExportJobCountsTest`, `…VerificationTest`, `…DestinationTest`, `…SideFilesTest`, `…ConsistencyTest`, `…RunStateTest`, each under 400 lines) over generated books (integration tier). An EPUB `<p>Tom &amp; <i>Jerry</i> ran.</p>` translated with the pseudo model writes `<p>TOM &amp; <i>JERRY</i> RAN.</p>` and `dc:language` `uk`; an EPUB briefed `en` → `uk` whose chapter declares `<html xml:lang="en">` is written with `xml:lang="uk"`; an FB2 `<lang>en</lang>` writes `<lang>uk</lang>`; a Markdown book with `lang: en` writes `lang: uk`, one with only `title: The Lighthouse` writes no `lang`; a TXT imported as windows-1252 whose target holds `Він відчинив старі двері.` is written whole as UTF-8 and re-opens intact; FLAGGED `He opened the *old* door.` with machine target `Він відчинив *старі* двері.` writes it and without one writes the source; REVISED `Він пішов геть.` over `Він пішов.` writes the edit; a PENDING `The end.` is written unchanged; exporting the same decisions to `a.epub` and `b.epub` gives canonical-equal books. Counts: 1,260 segments, 7 alt texts switched off, 312 body PENDING, 3 FLAGGED with a machine target, 922 accepted on their first draft, 16 REVISED → written 941, pending 312, sourceKept 7, flaggedWritten 3, autoAccepted 922, reviewed 16; adding one FLAGGED record with no machine target gives pending 313 and it is written as its source. Verification: a writer decorator whose output re-opens with 2 of 3 segments → `validation`, destination unchanged, no temporary file; one dropping `⟦g3⟧`'s fragment from `ch05.xhtml:11` → `validation` naming `ch5 · p12`; an EPUB paragraph `<p>Before <img src="fig1.png" alt="Figure 1"/> after</p>` whose alt text is translated as `Рисунок 1` verifies whether the paragraph is translated or PENDING (the latter written as `<p>Before <img src="fig1.png" alt="Рисунок 1" /> after</p>`); a Markdown `See ![Figure 1](fig1.png) below.` with alt `Рисунок 1` verifies and exports `![Рисунок 1](fig1.png)`; an EPUB whose NCX label `The Storm` differs from its navigation label `Storm`, both translated `Буря`, exports though its re-opened copy holds one auxiliary segment fewer; the destination folder deleted → the write error and no file left; source bytes changed after import → `validation`, nothing written. Destinations: the source path itself and `Kobzar.uk.fb2` for `Kobzar.fb2.zip` → `validation`; `Book.uk.markdown` for `Book.md` → accepted; `Book.uk.md` existing without overwrite → `validation` before any write; an occupied `Frankenstein.uk.glossary.csv` without overwrite → `validation`. `SideFileTest` (`:api`, parameterised): `/books/Frankenstein.uk.epub` with `EPUB` gives `/books/Frankenstein.uk.glossary.csv`, `.bilingual.html` and `.report.md`; `/books/Kobzar.uk.fb2.zip` with `FB2` gives `/books/Kobzar.uk.glossary.csv`; `/books/Kobzar.uk.fb2` with `FB2` the same; `/books/Book.uk.MD` with `MARKDOWN` gives `/books/Book.uk.report.md`; a destination the format does not match, `/books/Notes` with `EPUB`, gives `/books/Notes.glossary.csv`. Side files: all three for `Frankenstein.uk.epub` → `Frankenstein.uk.glossary.csv`, `.bilingual.html`, `.report.md` (for `Kobzar.uk.fb2.zip` → `Kobzar.uk.glossary.csv`), the CSV starting `term,target,type,gender,locked`, the HTML with one row per segment and no `http`; a failed verification with the report chosen writes neither book nor report. Consistency: `Justine` → `Джастін` held when chapter 2 was accepted, changed to `Жустіна` and locked → the pass on writes `Жустіна` and keeps the person's edited `Джастін прийшла.`; a `Джастін` chosen while `Justine` had no target stays. A run paused on `ErrorCode.upstream` stays paused with its counts when an export of its project fails; a pause requested on a finished run during export changes nothing.
  - **Log:** INFO the export's start (project id, destination, overwrite, side files, consistency pass) and end (outcome, counts, verification); DEBUG each check and step with its inputs, the languages passed to the writer and the kept kinds; WARN a refusal or a failed verification with its code and the named locator; ERROR an unexpected failure once.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:check` (including `ua.bookloom.pipeline.export.*`) and `./gradlew build` are green (`SideFile` is an `:api` shape; the window's interim bridge and the command line export through it).

- [x] 11.8 Apply the owner's decisions on the group 11 review findings: a FLAGGED segment that backward revision changes stays FLAGGED unless the change fixes every finding it carries, a revision call that waited through a pause is redone from the segment as it is after the pause, saving an edit or reverting withdraws the proposal built on the old wording, Retry drafts an oversized segment in pieces as the run does, and an export cancelled before anything was written says so. A name swap must not hide a real problem, a person's Revert, Retry or edit made during a pause must never be overwritten by an answer written for the old text, the panel must never offer text built on wording the person already changed, Retry must work on the longest paragraphs, and a log line must say only what happened. → `:pipeline`, `:api`, `:app`
  - **Depends on:** tasks 11.2, 11.4 and 11.5.
  - **Excludes:** rebuilding a waiting proposal when the same locked term is renamed twice — decided as a known limit (below); the review panel's display of proposals — tasks 14.5–14.7.
  - **Decisions (owner, after the group 11 review):**
    1. *Flagged paragraph and a name swap.* Example: `ch5 · p12` was FLAGGED because part of the sentence is missing (an `omission` finding); `Hale` changes from `Хейл` to `Гейл`. The swap replaces the name, and the segment stays FLAGGED, in the review list, with its `omission` finding — the swap did not fix that problem. A segment that carries at least one finding, all of them `glossary` findings, becomes REVISED after a name swap; a segment flagged with no finding, or with any other finding, stays FLAGGED. Findings do not name their term, so every `glossary` finding counts as about the swapped name. Findings are always kept on the record, as the history of what was wrong. Requirement "Move segments only through the segment status machine" says so.
    2. *Same locked term renamed twice* (`Хейл`→`Гейл`→`Гаїл` while a proposal for an edited segment waits): a known limit, not fixed. The waiting proposal keeps `Гейл`; the person ignores it or edits by hand. `RevisionBase`'s Javadoc and `design.md` D10 say so, so nobody assumes the proposal follows a second rename.
    3. *Pause during a revision call.* Example: the pass re-renders `ch2 · p12` for a gender, the person pauses, presses Revert (or Retry, or edits it), and resumes. The old answer is discarded and one new request is built from the segment as it reads after the pause; the person's action is never undone. The check and the write are one step, so nothing is stored for a segment that changed. The redo belongs to the gender re-render; the name sweep makes no model call, so a stale sweep leaves its deferrals open for the next pass.
    4. *Old proposal.* `saveEdit` and `revert` withdraw the segment's waiting proposal by resolving its carrier and adding it again without the proposal, so the deferral stays open and the next pass builds a new proposal from the new wording.
    5. *Retry on an oversized segment.* Retry computes the run's own chunk headroom (`run.ChunkBudget.headroom`) and cuts a segment the run drafted in pieces into the same pieces (`translateSplit`, as the run does) and joins them, instead of sending it whole.
    6. *Early export cancel.* A cancel before anything is written answers title `Export cancelled`, message `Nothing was written.` (the command prints `Export cancelled: Nothing was written.`); a cancel after the book was written and checked but before it is put in place answers `The translated book was checked but not published because the export was cancelled.`; a cancel after the book is in place but before every side file is written answers `The translated book was written, but the export was cancelled before all its side files were written.` All three keep `ErrorCode.cancelled`.
  - **Test first:** `RevisionWriterFlaggedTest` (in `ua.bookloom.pipeline.export`, so it reads the report through a real export; an `omission`-flagged `ch05.xhtml:11` holding `Хейл` swept to `Гейл` → target changed, still FLAGGED, finding kept, still in `queue(ALL_FLAGGED)` and in the report's flagged list; the same segment with only a `glossary` finding → REVISED); `ConsistencyPassPauseTest` (a gender re-render blocked on its request, the segment reverted or edited meanwhile, the pass resumed → a second request built from the new text and stored as that answer, not the first; a segment that changes during both calls stores nothing; the request is blocked directly rather than paused through `StageRunner`); `SegmentActionsProposalTest` (a proposal waiting on `ch02.xhtml:6`, then `saveEdit` → `queue`/`segment` show no proposal and the deferral is still open; the same after `revert`); `RetryDraftTest` gains an oversized segment split by the run and retried → several draft requests, one per piece, and the pieces joined; `ExportJobCancelTest` and `TranslateCommandShutdownTest` read the two messages.
  - **Log:** DEBUG the flagged-segment decision (findings kept or fixed, resulting status), the discarded answer and its reason, each withdrawn proposal, and the pieces a retry made; no book text above `TRACE`.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :pipeline:check :api:check :app:test` and `./gradlew clean build check spotlessCheck` are green.

## 12. UI plumbing — after group 11 (12.1 and 12.2 need nothing from groups 10–11)

- [x] 12.1 Add a searchable choice box, `ui.control.SearchableCombo<T>`, that narrows a fixed list to the entries whose displayed name contains the typed text anywhere, ignoring case and accents, and settles the typed text when focus leaves it in one of three modes: STRICT keeps the previous value when the text names no entry, FREE_TEXT keeps the typed text, PARSED hands the text to a caller's parser that may recognise a value the list does not hold. Without it a person scrolls a 34-entry language list and a 40-entry genre list; without STRICT a half-typed `Klingon` would become a target language; without PARSED a language the application can name but the list omits (`Latin`) could never be typed. → `:ui`
  - **Depends on:** nothing built by this change — only the existing `:ui` module.
  - **Excludes:** the language and genre lists, their display names and the parser that recognises a language by name or tag (12.2); wiring the combos into the Book Brief (13.2). ControlsFX `SearchableComboBox` is not used: it cannot take free text and is unproven in the packaged image (`design.md` D15).
  - **Read:** `specs/app-shell/spec.md` "Search any fixed list by typing"; `specs/book-brief/spec.md` "Offer one searchable list of languages for source and target" (its `Latin` and `Elvish` scenarios); `design.md` D1 (the `ua.bookloom.ui.control` package), D15; `docs/specification/01_Product/08_UI_SCREENS_AND_STATES.md#control-mapping-summary`; `modules/ui/src/main/java/module-info.java` (the `opens … to com.google.guice, javafx.fxml` lines to copy); `modules/ui/src/main/resources/ua/bookloom/ui/theme.css` (`.combo-box`, `.combo-box-popup`).
  - **Change:** create the package `ua.bookloom.ui.control` with a `package-info.java` (`@NullMarked`, a purpose Javadoc) and the line `opens ua.bookloom.ui.control to com.google.guice, javafx.fxml;` in `module-info.java`. `SearchableCombo<T>` extends an editable `ComboBox<T>` over a `FilteredList<T>` whose predicate compares the editor text with each entry's displayed name after `java.text.Normalizer` NFD decomposition, removal of combining marks (`\p{M}`) and `Locale.ROOT` lower-casing. One static factory per mode, so no caller can pair a mode with the wrong function: `strict(items, Function<T,String> name)`, `freeText(items, name, Function<String,T> fromText)`, `parsed(items, name, Function<String,Optional<T>> parse)`. The committed value is a read-only property; a read-only `invalidText` property (`@Nullable String`) holds the text the box last refused and is cleared by the next commit. On focus loss STRICT commits the entry whose normalized name equals the text, otherwise restores the value held before typing began and sets `invalidText`; FREE_TEXT commits `fromText(text)`; PARSED commits the parser's value when present, even one the list does not hold, otherwise restores and sets `invalidText`. Choosing an entry from the narrowed list commits it and clears the filter. No new colour: it reuses `.combo-box`.
  - **Test first:** `SearchableComboTest` (TestFX, headless). A `@ParameterizedTest` over (`typed`, `expectedFirst`) on the 34 English language names written out in the test (not read from `Languages`): `ukr` leaves exactly `Ukrainian`; `fren` includes `French`; `BOKMAL` includes `Norwegian Bokmål`; `chin` leaves `Chinese (Simplified)` and `Chinese (Traditional)`. One test per mode: STRICT with `Ukrainian` chosen, typing `xyz` and moving focus away leaves `Ukrainian` and `invalidText` reads `xyz`; FREE_TEXT typing `solarpunk novella` and moving focus away commits `solarpunk novella`; PARSED over the list `English`, `Polish`, `Ukrainian` with a test parser that maps `Latin` and `la` to `la`: typing `Latin` and moving focus away commits `la`, a value the list does not hold; typing `Elvish` with `Polish` chosen keeps `Polish` and sets `invalidText` to `Elvish`, and choosing `Ukrainian` afterwards clears it; choosing an entry from the narrowed list commits it and empties the filter.
  - **Log:** DEBUG once per commit with the mode and where the value came from (list, free text, parser, restored); nothing per keystroke.
  - **Done when:** `./gradlew :ui:test --tests 'ua.bookloom.ui.control.*'` and `./gradlew :ui:check :app:archTest` are green.

Importing {new} stops that run and discards its progress.`; for a stopped run `{run} was stopped and can be resumed. Importing {new} discards its progress.`; buttons `Keep translation` (`btn-secondary`) and `Discard and import` (`btn-danger-outline`); after confirming an active run the card shows `Stopping the run…` with both buttons disabled until `dismiss()`. Ukrainian: `Замінити поточний переклад?`; `Триває переклад файлу {0}. Імпорт {1} зупинить його й відкине прогрес.`; `Переклад файлу {0} зупинено, його можна відновити. Імпорт {1} відкине прогрес.`; `Залишити переклад`, `Відкинути й імпортувати`, `Зупиняємо переклад…`. `ui.state.ImportGuard` (`@Singleton`, constructor-injected `StateMirror`, `TranslationRunner`, `ImportViewModel`, `ReplaceRunPrompt`) has `requestImport(Path)`, which `ImportController` calls from both `openDropped` and `chooseFile` in place of `viewModel.open`. IDLE opens at once. COMPLETED and FAILED: `mirror.publishRunCleared()` (new: run state IDLE, `runFileName` null, figures, log, failure and report reset), then open, with no dialog. Any other state asks. On confirm of STOPPED — the job has already ended and the runner is free — it clears the run state, calls `ImportViewModel.cancel()` (which calls `ProjectService.close`) and then `open`. On confirm of an active state (including PAUSED, because a paused job still holds the runner) it calls `runner.cancel()`, waits without blocking the FX thread by observing `mirror.runState()` until it is STOPPED, COMPLETED or FAILED, then dismisses the dialog, clears, cancels the import and opens. Keep translation does nothing: the run and the open book are untouched. New keys `dialog.replaceRun.*` in both catalogues.

A `@ParameterizedTest` over RUNNING, PAUSED and provider-error PAUSED: the prompt is asked once naming `Frankenstein.epub` and `Dracula.epub`, nothing is imported yet, confirming makes `RecordingJob.calls()` contain exactly one `cancel`, and once the job returns cancelled the prompt is dismissed once, `close("p1")` is recorded before `importBook(Dracula.epub)`, and `runState` reads IDLE. STOPPED: confirming records no `cancel`, then `close("p1")` and `importBook`. Keep translation, for each: no `importBook`, no `close`, no `cancel`, `runState` unchanged. `ReplaceRunDialogTest` (TestFX, patterned on `AboutDialogTest`): the active text, the stopped text and both buttons render for `Frankenstein.epub` and `Dracula.epub`; `Keep translation` hides the host and never runs the confirm action; confirm runs it once and, for an active run, shows `Stopping the run…` with both buttons disabled; `dismiss()` hides the host; card fill and border resolve to the `surface` and `border` roles in both blocks (`ThemeTestSupport.assertSameColour`); under Ukrainian at the 944 px content width no button label ends in `…`. `ImportScreenTest`: with a paused run, `openDropped(Dracula.epub)` asks the prompt and calls no `importBook`.

## 14. Names & style and Translating — after group 13

## 15. Export and Settings — after group 14 (all four tasks below edit the same message catalogues, `modules/ui/src/main/resources/ua/bookloom/ui/theme.css` and `ScreenConformanceTest`'s cases as group 14 does, so group 15 never runs alongside it; 15.4 needs nothing from 15.1–15.3 in code, and follows them only because they share those files)

- [x] 15.1 Give the export viewmodel everything the screen states before a book is written: the replace switch, the side-file choices, the consistency switch, the refusals beside Save to for the source itself, a changed file type and an occupied book or side-file path, whether Export is available, and the partial-export statement that names pending segments and segments kept as source by choice apart. The run no longer writes the book (ADR-0035); a count that lumps switched-off text in with untranslated text would make a finished book look unfinished. → `:ui`
  - **Depends on:** task 13.2 (`ExportViewModel`, holding the destination members moved out of `BookBriefViewModel` — the proposal from `DestinationPath.destinationFor`, the hand-edited path kept across target changes, `overwrite`, the off-thread existence check — and the brief's target language and quality dial); task 12.3 (`CurrentProject`: the project id and the source path); task 11.2 (`ReviewDeskImpl.counts`); task 11.5 (`ExportJobImpl`, which writes the side files, and `SideFile.pathBeside`); task 11.6 (the recording `ScriptedReviewDesk`); the mirror's `RunState`; `QueuedExecutor`.
  - **Excludes:** running the export, its outcome and the dialog (task 15.2); the view (task 15.3); Settings (task 15.4).
  - **Read:** `specs/export/spec.md` "Choose where to save on the export screen", "Replace an existing file only when the person allows it", "Refuse a destination the book cannot be written to", "Export at any time except while a run is translating", "State what a partial export will write before it is written", "Write the side files that are chosen beside the book", "Run the final consistency pass before writing when it is switched on"; `specs/review-queue/spec.md` "Allow export whatever remains flagged, stating the count"; `design.md` D13 (side-file names, the report's counts); `docs/specification/01_Product/08_UI_SCREENS_AND_STATES.md#screen-export`; `modules/api/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/api/pipeline/{SideFile,ReviewCounts,QualityDial}.java`; `modules/util/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/util/paths/DestinationPath.java`; `modules/ui/src/main/java/ua/bookloom/ui/state/ExportViewModel.java` and its tests as task 13.2 left them.
  - **Change:** `ExportViewModel` names each chosen side file through `SideFile.pathBeside(destination, format)`, the rule task 11.5 created for `ExportJobImpl` (`format` is the source file's, from `BookFormat.ofFileName`), so `:ui` and `:pipeline` share one rule (`:ui` cannot see `:pipeline`'s internal packages). `ExportViewModel` adds: the replace switch (off), the side-file set (glossary on), the consistency switch (on exactly when the brief's dial is `MAX`); a pure `ui.state.ExportPathRules` deciding the refusals beside Save to — the destination equal to the source, and a changed file type (`.fb2.zip` ≠ `.fb2`; `.md` and `.markdown` are the same type) — and the existing off-thread existence check widened to the book path and each chosen side file's `pathBeside` path, refusing with replace off and naming the occupied path; `exportAvailable`, false while the run is RUNNING, PAUSING or STOPPING (note `Pause the run to export`) or an export is in progress, and while any refusal stands; and the partial-export statement from `ReviewDesk.counts(projectId)`: its `pending` plus its `flaggedWithoutTarget` (a pending count never includes a segment kept as source by choice) as `<n> segments will be written in the source language`, its `flagged` minus `flaggedWithoutTarget` as `<n> flagged segments will be written with their machine translation` (a count of zero states no line), and, when `sourceKept` is above zero, `<n> segments are kept as source by choice` — so a book with no run states every translated segment as source. ## 15b. Real-book hardening — after the hand test

The Bartimaeus run (≈3,700 segments, Ollama `gemma4:26b-mlx`) stalled on a re-judge that hung for the full timeout seven
times, paused, and on every resume paid the same rounds again. Each step is its own commit, tests first.

- [x] 1. Stop the stall: the judge call capped by its pairs and its schema bounded; per-kind timeouts (judge 90 s,
  prescan and summary 120 s); a timed-out call retried once with a seed and a lower cap, never three identical
  resends; the Ollama reply read as a stream with a 60 s idle gap; a judge that times out or cannot be reached flags its
  segments (`judge-unavailable`) instead of pausing; a step that pauses twice is flagged and the run goes on, and a
  paused run can skip the failing segment (`TranslationJob.skipSegment`); rounds that make no progress stop early; a
  resume continues at the call that failed (380db02, fa83b1c)
- [x] 2. Make the run observable: per-attempt model-call events (`CallAttemptListener` in `:api`, reported by the gated
  model, announced by `JobModelCalls` with timeout and request size; a failed attempt finishes with its code; the
  chunk's judge names its segments), `ContextAssembled` and `RoundStarted`; the waiting banner naming the call, segment
  and attempt with its own clock against the timeout and the call's total, turning into a warning with Skip segment /
  Retry now / Pause after a minute; the provider-error banner naming the failed call, the pauses spent and what Retry
  now does, with Skip segment, and no "no work was lost" claim; a connection chip in the title bar; a timed activity
  log with call lines, repeats counted and an errors-only view; the round tracker and the collapsed context section in
  the live row; the run controls under the banner; no empty live boxes and a worded blank source in review (c227759)
- [x] 3. Segments: one shared visible-text test (`VisibleText` in `:util`: separators, controls and format characters
  are invisible) so a no-break-space, zero-width or soft-hyphen-only run, paragraph or alt text is no segment in any
  format; a segment with no letter, an upper-case Roman numeral, a single character or only a locked name is ACCEPTED
  as it is with the path `VERBATIM` and no call, judge, finding or memory entry, counted apart (`keptVerbatim` on the
  progress, the review counts and the export report; its own export-statement line and outcome tile) and left out of
  the time-left average; a translation call's cap never below 128 tokens; a short echo of glossary names only is not
  held against the target; an identical auxiliary text takes the chunk's undecided draft or the run's accepted target
  instead of a call (2dc1bfc)
- [x] 4. Glossary: one key (`GlossaryKeys` in `:util`: NFC, edge punctuation and a trailing possessive dropped, case
  folded) for the repository, the scans, Add term and the CSV import; the frequency scan drops a word written in lower
  case 20% of the time or more, reads a bundled stop-word list per source language (English fallback), keeps
  apostrophe and hyphen words whole, treats a dash or a quotation mark as a sentence start, counts a sentence-initial
  word seen mid-sentence twice, and leaves out a word mostly seen inside a longer name; "Review with model"
  (`REVIEW_TERMS`, 40 terms a call with counts and examples) removes what it judges not a name and fills an unset type
  or gender, changing nothing unless every call answered; both model actions go through `JobModelCalls` with a waiting
  line and Stop; the glossary table sorts by Term, Type, Gender and Locked (ties by term) and has a search field
  (5b2c5fa)
- [x] 5. Scrolling smoothness: a wheel notch glides over 150 ms (`SmoothScroll`, installed once on the shell root,
  moves the innermost pane, list, table or tree under the pointer that still has room, adds up fast notches, answers a
  reversal at once, stops on a mouse press; trackpad, momentum and touch events are left to JavaFX); the glossary's
  Type and Gender are text that becomes a combo box on click, focus or Enter, its row tooltips arrive on the first
  hover, and its switches draw no thumb shadow until focused; the activity log, the review list and the structure tree
  have a fixed row height, and a review row reuses its nodes; `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` says how to measure with the pulse
  logger, and no JVM flag was added (8a3d801)
- [x] 6. UI polish from the hand-test screenshots: the file just exported is named beside Save to in a neutral line,
  not the red refusal, and Export book still waits for Replace; the export-complete dialog shows the whole folder and
  file path on hover; an unchosen check box paints no tick (the light tick showed on the dark theme's box); the time
  left is the average of the last 20 timed segments, still hidden before 5; the import card writes `EPUB 2.0` once; an
  empty target language says it is required; an available Settings tab reads as ordinary text and an unavailable one
  as muted. Not done: a Structure note on segments kept as they are — the structure counts come from the inspection,
  before any run decides what is verbatim, so it cannot be derived from data the screen holds (eec2c45)

## 15c. Second real-run round — after group 15b

A second Bartimaeus run found more problems (plan: export refused as a whole, placeholders lost on drop caps, the
review panel not taking edits while running, scrolling and the squeezed context panel, overlapping model actions, the
prompts, names without suggested targets). Each step is its own commit, tests first; step 0 comes first so every later
check leaves evidence.

- [x] 0. Shareable full-trace diagnostic log: `bookloom-trace.log` beside `bookloom.log` (TRACE for `ua.bookloom`, one
  line per event, MDC job/segment, 20 MB × 5 gzip), on by default in a development run and with
  `BOOKLOOM_TRACE_FILE=1` / `-Dbookloom.trace.file=true` installed, the ordinary log's level unchanged; a `#` session
  header at the top of each file and after every rotation (version, OS, JVM, JavaFX, locale, levels, and the
  `SessionInfo` facts the window records — provider, kind, endpoint host, model, dial, review mode, brief, book file
  name, format and size — each change an INFO `session update` line); INFO pause/resume/stop requests, boundary pauses
  and how each pause ended, glossary scan start/end and pre-scan failure; a `RunSummaryLogger` line at most once a
  minute and at the end; a redaction guard over both provider clients at TRACE; About says whether the detailed log is
  on and that it holds book text and stays local, names the folder, and offers Open log folder, Copy log path and Save

## 15d. Translation-quality engineering — after group 15c (evals first; each task adds its eval cases and must move a metric; one gradle at a time)

**Execution order (revised 2026-10-04):** 15d.0, 15d.1 (done), 15d.2, 15d.3, 15d.4, 15d.5, 15d.5b, then 15d.8 (the batch protocol), then 15d.6 (the reviewer shares that protocol), 15d.7, 15d.9, 15d.10, 15d.11, 15d.12, 15d.13, 15d.14. Local inference stays single-flight (the `InferenceGate`); chapter-level parallelism for cloud providers is a possible later change, not a task here. Language rules are injected per source/target pair only (15d.5b).

- [x] 15d.0 Measure first, before any speed claim: log per call kind the prompt tokens, output tokens, prompt-eval time and generation time (Ollama `prompt_eval_count/duration`, `eval_count/duration`; LM Studio response `stats`/`usage`), add a read-only segment-length histogram tool, and run 300 Bartimaeus segments on gemma4:e4b and the 26b class to record where the wall time goes (prompt evaluation vs generation, by call kind) and how much the server's prompt cache already saves. → `:llm`, `:pipeline`, scripts, docs
  - **Depends on:** nothing (can run beside 15d.1).
  - **Read:** `OllamaReplyReader`, `OpenAiCompatibleClient`, `ChatResponse`, `RunSummaryLogger`, `scripts/e2e-fixture.sh`; the backlog section "Real-run follow-ups, 2026-10-03" (5,662 calls: draft 52.7%, judge 34.8%, fix 11.4%).
  - **Change:** `ChatResponse` carries optional timing/usage; the run summary line and the report JSON add per-kind tokens and durations; `scripts/segment-histogram.py` (words per block of an EPUB/FB2/MD/TXT: mean, median, share of blocks and of words at 1-3, 4-10, 11-30, over 30 words).
  - **Test first:** parsing of both dialects' timing fields (WireMock); the histogram on a fixture.
  - **Done when:** `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` has a table "where the time goes" (per call kind: calls, input tokens, output tokens, prompt-eval share, generation share) and the segment-length table (Bartimaeus 1: 3,576 blocks, median 24 words, 29% of blocks at most 10 words but about 4% of the words; Harry Potter 1: 2,917 blocks, median 18 words), and the 15d.14 targets are restated from these numbers.

- [x] 15d.1 Build the eval foundation first: a labelled regression corpus of synthetic paraphrases of the defects the 8-hour run exposed (garbled word, `навчg3`-style mixed script, unbalanced « », English paragraph accepted, false-alarm idiom flagged, judge glossary hallucination, gender slip, lexical drift, junk names, length false positive) plus per-prompt eval suites (draft, judge, directed fix, reflect/improve, summary, prescan/review/suggest) with metrics, a stability run (every case ×5 at temperature 0 must give the same verdict) and `scripts/eval-matrix.sh` over Ollama and LM Studio models (e2b, e4b, 12b, 26b class), because every later change needs a number to move. → `:pipeline` (test sources, `promptEval`), scripts, docs
  - **Depends on:** the `promptEval` harness (step 9) and `scripts/e2e-fixture.sh`; nothing else.
  - **Read:** `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` promptEval section; `modules/pipeline/src/test/.../promptEval`; the backlog section "Real-run follow-ups, 2026-10-03".
  - **Change:** corpus under `modules/pipeline/src/test/resources/eval/` (JSON cases: id, kind, source, candidate/expected, labels, rationale; synthetic text only — no book excerpts beyond a few words); metrics per suite (parse rate, gate pass, false-positive and false-negative rate of the judge, verdict stability, glossary adherence, quote balance, script purity, omission rate); JSON + markdown report per model; thresholds per model class in one file; matrix script prints a comparison table.
  - **Test first:** the corpus loader and metric calculators on fixed inputs (no model); stability metric on a scripted flip-flopping fake.
  - **Done when:** `scripts/eval-matrix.sh` reports baseline numbers for the current prompts on e4b and the 26b-class model, committed to `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` as the "before" table.

- [x] 15d.2 Add a deterministic `TextChecks` library that catches what code can decide: per-word script purity (any Latin letter or digit inside a target-script word, look-alike letters, e.g. `навчg3`, `імпoву`), direct-speech quote and bracket pair balance driven by a per-language convention table (only quote characters, open/close alternation, nesting depth, compared with the source's pairs — never general punctuation, which differs by language), language identity for long segments (bundled stop-word lists: a paragraph of ≥ 12 words whose source-language stop-word rate stays high or whose target-script letter share is under 60% is a hard failure, the silent English paragraph), adjacent duplicate words, spacing artefacts (` .`, double spaces; the footnote-marker spacing waits for 15d.3, the normalizer that sees the markers), and a recalibrated length check (no omission flag for compact short lines). Each finding carries the exact span and a plain explanation the fix prompt reuses. → `:pipeline`, `:util`
  - **Depends on:** 15d.1 (corpus cases).
  - **Read:** `CheckName`, `LengthCheck`, `EchoCheck`, `ScriptCheck`, `AcceptanceRule`, `glossary/StopWords`, quality-gates spec.
  - **Change:** new `checks.TextChecks` with one small class per check (script purity, quote balance, language identity, duplicate word, spacing; the recalibrated length check stays in `qa.LengthCheck`, next to its band and weight) returning typed `CheckFinding(kind, span, explanation, blocking)`; blocking: mixed-script word, unbalanced quote pair that the source balanced, leftover source-language paragraph; soft: duplicate word, spacing, length; replace the old length/omission rule's false positives (14 in the run); skip script/echo checks for digit/URL/ISBN-only text (copyright lines flagged "wrong lang?"); wire into the soft/hard gates before any model judge; findings feed `DirectedFix` with the span.
  - **Test first:** `навчg3`, `імпoву`, `розлізяв` (not detectable — documented), `«Ні, сер, — відповів хлопчик.` (missing close), `«Зупинись!» — крикнув він. — Геть!»` (stray close), `part0044:16`-style English paragraph, `одно одно таки`, the 14 length false positives, an ISBN/URL line; both Ukrainian and one non-Cyrillic target (Polish) to prove language neutrality.
  - **Log:** DEBUG each check with its inputs and decision, WARN a hard failure with the span (span only; text at TRACE).
  - **Done when:** the corpus recall for these classes is 100% with no false positive on the fixture book translated by e4b, and the English-leftover case is a hard failure.

- [x] 15d.3 Add a deterministic typography normalizer applied before judging and at export: straight→typographic apostrophes (88% were straight), `...`→`…`, primary and nested quote style and dialogue dash per the target language's convention table (default: keep the source's convention — quoted speech becomes «», dash dialogue stays dash), space before punctuation, never inside placeholders, code spans or kept-foreign spans. → `:pipeline` (`:document` is untouched: the normaliser works on masked targets, so a no-op reassembly never sees it). Spacing before footnote markers is deferred: a marker is an ordinary `⟦gN⟧` token and the masked text cannot say which token is a footnote, so it waits for a task that carries marker roles.
  - **Depends on:** 15d.2 (convention table).
  - **Change:** `typography.TypographyNormalizer` with a language table (resource files; uk first, neutral fallback that only fixes apostrophes/ellipsis/spacing); applied to every accepted target and recorded as a low-severity "normalised" note; export re-applies to edits; setting-free.
  - **Test first:** apostrophe/ellipsis/quote cases incl. `«підлий демоне»»` (nested), idempotence (normalise twice = once), tokens untouched, TXT exact-bytes golden unaffected for source text.
  - **Done when:** on the fixture and on the corpus no straight apostrophe or `...` remains in uk output and the golden round-trip stays green.

- [x] 15d.4 Harden the glossary scan and review against junk: scan only body text (exclude front/back matter detected from landmarks and file roles, headings, metadata, the author and publisher lines), drop ALL-CAPS tokens and boilerplate/ad-copy (T-shirt, Yellow Pages, CROYDON, Hyperion Books), give the review prompt frequency, an example sentence and the requirement to quote evidence for "not-a-name", validate empty targets before Start translation (confirm dialog listing the entries without a target), and add a junk-likelihood sort. → `:api` (`UnitRole` on `Unit`), `:util` (`JunkRules`), `:document` (roles from landmarks, guide, `epub:type`, contents labels, file names, first line; FB2 `notes`/`comments` bodies), `:pipeline`, `:ui`
  - **Depends on:** 15d.1.
  - **Read:** `FrequencyScan`, `ScanText`, `TermReview`, `ReviewCommit`, `NamesStyleView`, `GlossaryTable`.
  - **Change:** landmark/role-based exclusion in `ScanText`; `JunkRules`; review schema gets `evidence`; the UI marks rows "no target" and "likely junk"; Start shows 'N entries have no target — the model will choose'.
  - **Test first:** a fixture extension with the real junk kinds (T-shirt, Yellow Pages, CROYDON, author name, ad lines) and expected survivors; review reply parsing with evidence; the confirm dialog (TestFX).
  - **Built as:** the junk extension is added in memory to the opened fixture book (a front unit, a story unit and a back unit built in the test) rather than regenerated into `make-fixtures.py`, which would move every golden and soak expectation; FB2 has no section roles, so only its `notes`/`comments` bodies are back matter; the author name is dropped by the front/back-matter and printing-note rules, not by matching the metadata author (deferred: a book whose story text names its own author).
  - **Done when:** the fixture scan proposes only the eight real names plus intended terms and the Bartimaeus first-chapter scan has no entry from the list above.
  - **Open: owner decision (2026-10-05 review):** `JunkRules` drops every ALL-CAPS token, which also drops acronym names (FBI, KGB, HAL, R2-D2), and the note-term rule drops a name such as 'Penguin'; both are left as built until the owner chooses.

- [x] 15d.5 Introduce the `ContextBudget` and prompt hygiene: detect the usable context from Ollama `/api/show` and LM Studio's model endpoint (cap and override constant, default effective 8k), allocate tokens by priority (chunk text → glossary/lexicon for terms present → preceding source+target pairs of the same chapter → summary → examples), dedupe, drop empty sections and "(none)" filler lines, keep the static prefix (system prompt, style sheet) byte-identical across calls so servers can reuse their prompt cache, log a per-section token breakdown at DEBUG and `prompt_eval_duration` when the server reports it, and add a prompt-hygiene lint test over real assembled prompts for every call kind. The window formula: usable window W minus the static prefix (system, style sheet, examples, language rules; byte-identical, about 900 tokens or less), minus the dynamic context (glossary and lexicon for terms present, the last 2-3 source→target pairs of the same chapter, summary, who-is-who; about 1,100 or less), minus a safety margin (500), is split between a batch's source tokens T and its output r·T, r from `LengthBand` for the language pair (uk about 1.5); at 8k that allows about 2,200 source tokens, so the real ceiling is quality: the batch size per model class is an output of a promptEval sweep (4/8/12/16 items, e4b and the 26b class) and a run-time controller halves it on id, omission or merge failures and grows it slowly after clean streaks. → `:pipeline`, `:llm`
  - **Built in 15d.5:** the `ModelCapabilities` port with both dialects' detection, `ContextBudget` (window, prefix, dynamic allowance, chunk and reply sizes) driving chunk packing and the dynamic-context cut, `PromptHygiene` inside `DraftContext`, the DEBUG breakdown, the lint and the unwired `BatchSizeController`. **Deferred to 15d.8's first step:** carrying the detected window into a run (a `RunRequest` window, `ModelCapabilities` called when a run is created, `num_ctx` taken from the budget instead of the 8192 default, and `max_tokens`/`num_predict` from `ContextBudget.outputTokens`); until then a run uses the 8192 default and the budget math is tested at 4k, 8k and 32k.
  - **Depends on:** 15d.1.
  - **Read:** `TokenBudget`, `TokenEstimator`, `ContextPackageAssembler`, `InjectedTerms`, `CallFrame`, `PromptTemplates`, `OllamaClient`, `OpenAiCompatibleClient`, `ProviderVerifierImpl`.
  - **Change:** `context.ContextBudget` replaces ad-hoc limits; sections declare priority, minimum and relevance rule (glossary only for terms present; preceding pairs only same chapter; summary only when it exists); `num_ctx`/`max_tokens` derived from it; provider capability port for context length; trace of the breakdown; no garbage rule documented in `12_PROMPT_CATALOG.md`.
  - **Test first:** budget allocation tables (tiny/8k/32k contexts); hygiene lint failing on an empty heading, a duplicate line, a term not present in the chunk, a previous-chapter pair; byte-identical prefix across two calls; capability parsing on both dialects (WireMock).
  - **Done when:** the lint passes for every `PromptName`, the breakdown shows the static prefix share per call kind, and a model with a 4k context is never sent a prompt over its budget.

- [x] 15d.5b Add the language-rules map, injected per language pair: one bundled file per language (`prompt/languages/<tag>.properties`), optional pair files (`prompt/languages/pairs/<source>-<target>.properties`) and `generic.properties`; the prompt gets a single section `[Language rules: <Source> -> <Target>]` built from the target language's rules, a few source-language notes and the pair's rules, and no other language's rules are ever loaded. First version: English, Russian, Ukrainian, French, Croatian, Polish, Czech, Slovenian, Slovak, Spanish, Portuguese, German; every other BCP-47 tag gets the generic rules and keeps working (languages are open). Rule keys (all optional): `quotes`, `dialogue`, `apostrophe`/`hyphen`/`ellipsis`, `agreement` (gender, number, case, aspect, past-tense verb gender), `address` (T-V), `numbers`/`dates`, `pitfalls.N` (what a model gets wrong in this language), `sourceNotes.N` (what to read carefully when it is the source, e.g. English has no gender in "I/you/they"), `reviewerChecks.N`, `example` (replaces `prompt/examples/*.txt`) and non-prompt data (script, RTL flag, stop words, quote pairs for the 15d.2 balance check, an optional check hook such as `GenderCheck`). Pair files hold what exists only for the pair (ru→uk russisms and false friends, hr/sl/sk/cs/pl false friends, gender recovery en→uk/ru/pl, gender loss uk→en, name transliteration, absorbing `name-rendering.properties`). Size limits enforced by the 15d.5 lint: target rules about 200 tokens, source about 80, pair about 100. Each language ships a mini-corpus in the 15d.1 format (draft and reviewer cases), and a rule list stays only if the matrix shows it moves a metric against the generic rules on e4b and the 26b class; a language without a corpus is shown as "untested" in the UI. Rules for languages the owner does not read are drafted for native-speaker review before they are marked tested. → `:pipeline`, `:util`, `:ui` (the untested note), docs
  - **Depends on:** 15d.1, 15d.5 (the lint and budget).
  - **Read:** `PromptTemplates`, `PromptExamples`, `StylePhrases`, `PromptLanguages`, `CallFrame`, `NameRules`, `prompt/examples/*.txt`, `name-rendering.properties`, `util` language catalogue.
  - **Change:** `LanguageRules` (loader and per-pair assembler, cached per run), `{{languageRules}}` slot in the draft, reviewer, summary, prescan and suggest-targets templates, removal of the per-language example files and the multi-language advice from the static prompts, the 15d.2/15d.10 consumers read quote tables, stop words and gender hooks from this map instead of hard-coding Ukrainian; spec `12_PROMPT_CATALOG.md`.
